PREFACE 
 
 I, the Chairman of the Department-related Parliamentary Standing Committee on 
Health and Family Welfare, having been authorized by the Committee to present the 
Report on its behalf, do hereby present this Thirty-ninth Report of the Committee on the 
Demands for Grants (Demand No.46) of the De partment of Health and Family Welfare, 
Ministry of Health and Family Welfare for the year 2010-11. 
2. The Committee considered the various documents and relevant papers received 
from the Department of Health and Family Welfare, Ministry of Health and Family 
Welfare and also heard the Secretary and other officials of that Department on the said 
Demands for Grants (2010-11) in its meetings held on the 23rd March and 6th April, 2010. 
3. The Committee considered the Draft Report and adopted the same in its meeting 
held on the 26th April, 2010. 
 
 
NEW DELHI;                                                                                     Amar Singh 
   
                   AMAR SINGH 
26
th April, 2010                                                                            Chairman, 
Vaishakha 7, 1932 (saka)                                    Department-related Parliamentary 
                                                                Standing Committee on Health and Family Welfare 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
(ii)

PART – A        
HEALTH SECTOR 
 
II   BUDGETARY ALLOCATION 
2.1 The Ministry had projected a demand  for Rs. 26985.00 crore to the Planning 
Commission for the Department of Health an d Family Welfare (including funds for the 
Department of AIDS Control) for the Annu al Plan 2010-11. As against the proposed 
outlay, the Planning Commission approved an outlay of Rs. 21000.00 crore, indicating a 
shortfall of Rs. 5985 crore. The proposed an d approved outlays for the Department of 
Health and Family Welfare work out to Rs . 25720 crore and Rs. 19565 crore respectively. 
Keeping in view the inter-sectoral priorities  within the Department of Health & Family 
Welfare, the distribution of outlay between  NRHM and Health Schemes was fixed at Rs. 
15440 crore and Rs. 4125 crore respectively.  
 
2.2 The Committee was informed that this re duction in outlay was likely to affect 
implementation of a number of ongoing sc hemes in the Health Sector like Pradhan 
Mantri Swasthya Suraksha Yojana (PMSSY ), Oversight Committee, Upgradation of 
Government Medical Colleges, Upgradation/Strengthening of Nursing Schools/Colleges 
and revised strategy for implementation of Cancer Control and Mental Health 
Programme. Some new initiatives like National Programme for Cardio Vascular Diseases 
& Stroke, Strengthening/Creation of Parame dical Institutions and Health Care for 
Elderly may also require additional funds during  the year 2010-11. To offset the effect of 
shortfall of funds, a mid-year exercise is prop osed to be carried out by the Department to 
assess the requirement of fund s under the schemes/programmes based on the activities 
planned and progress made therein and addi tional funds would be sought from the 
Ministry of Finance accordingly. 
2.3 Against an approved Outlay of Rs. 41092. 92 crore for the Eleventh Plan, allocation 
during the first four years of the plan period  shows a gradual increase. However, pace of 
utilization so far has not matched with the allocated funds as indicated below: 
 

 7
 (Rs. In crores) 
2007-08 2008-09 2009-10 2010-11 
BE Actuals BE Actuals BE Actuals 
(Upto 28.2.2010)

2985.00 2183.71 3650.00 3008.22 4450.00 2559.61 5560.00 
 
2.4 With this trend of allocation of funds and utilization thereof, the Committee 
does not have much hope about optimum release of funds out of the approved outlay 
of Rs. 41092.92 crore in the remaining two years of the Eleventh Plan.  The Department 
will have to make concerted efforts so as to ensure optimum utilization of plan 
allocation of Rs. 5560.00 crore in 2010-11.  On ly then, there are chances of enhanced 
provision being made in 2011-12, the last year of the Eleventh Plan. 
2.5 The Committee in its 27
th Report on Demands for Grants (2008-09) had 
commented on the Department’s inability to project its budgetary requirements in an 
effective way for the simple reason that th e utilization of funds during the Tenth Plan 
was far from satisfactory. The Committee finds that the same trend continues in the 
Eleventh Plan period also. During 2007-08 a nd 2009-10,  keeping in view the pace of 
expenditure, allocated funds were substantiall y reduced at the RE stage. What is more 
worrisome is that the Department could not achieve optimum utilization of this 
reduced allocation during these two years.  Enhanced Plan allocation of Rs. 4450.00 in 
2009-10 failed to serve the purpose as it had to be brought down to Rs. 3825.25 crore, 
marginally lower than the 2008-09 allocation of Rs. 3650.00 crore.  Utilization status in 
2009-10 was more disappointing.  As on 28
th February, 2010, expenditure reported was 
Rs. 2559.61 crore which is less than the utilization of Rs. 3008.40 crore in the previous 
year. The Committee also takes note of th e fact that expenditure reported upto 31
st 
January 2010 under Plan head was only Rs. 2034. 06 crore. This clearly points to the fact 
that major utilization is taking place in th e last quarter of the financial year, thus 
indicating non-adherence to fiscal discipline. 
 2.6 The Committee’s examination of the following major heads of account has 
revealed recurrent and persistent sub-optimal utilization of budgetary provisions 
(Plan) during the Eleventh Plan period:-

2.7 The Committee is dismayed at the fact that its continuous concerns and 
recommendations to the Department to put a proper mechanism in place for - 
presenting its case for fund requirement before the Planning Commission  in a 
realistic manner, and to arrest the trend of under utilization of funds seem to have 
failed to make any impact.  Atleast the Department needs to try some innovative 
methods to achieve optimum utilization of funds. Health is a crucial sector for the 
allround development of the nation and accordingly the Committee has been 
advocating higher allocation of funds for this sector every year.  But with the 
Department itself admitting to the slow pace of expenditure in respect of many 
schemes/programmes initiated by it, the Committee finds advocacy for additional 
funds serving no purpose. The Committee, therefore, recommends to the Department 
to undertake a comprehensive analysis of th e trend of expenditure for the past three 
years with a view to identifying the underlying causes of underutilization. Such an 
exercise should enable the Department to no t only improve the utilization level of the 
budgeted funds but also to improve upon the existing system of assessing requirement 
of funds. The Committee is also of the opinion that the Department needs to take up 
with the implementing agencies as and when schemes are found not being 
implemented as per the Action Plan.  
 
2.8 The Committee desires to be apprised  of the necessary action initiated and 
pursued by the Department to streamline the formulation of budgetary requirements 

 9
in a realistic fashion and also to expedite necessary approvals for operationalization of 
various formulated schemes/projects. 
 
2.9 The Committee is specially concerned with  the slow pace of implementation of 
schemes like Pradhan Mantri Swasthya Sur aksha Yojna (PMSSY), Cancer Research, 
National Mental Health Programme, Assistance for Capacity Building for Trauma 
Centers, Oversight Committee, Upgradat ion/strengthening of Nursing Services, 
Strengthening/Upgradation of Pharmacy schools/colleges, Strengthening/creation of 
paramedical institutions as well as underutilization of allocated funds by some of the 
Hospitals/institutions. The Committee observes that this recurrent and persistent 
trend of underutilization is indicative of ineffectiveness of monitoring of 
implementation of schemes and utilization of funds.  The Committee therefore 
recommends to the Department to keep the activities of these schemes/institutions 
under constant scrutiny/monitoring and take urgent remedial steps so that the 
stumbling blocks in the optimum utilization of allocated/sanctioned funds are 
removed. 
2.10 Committee’s attention has also been drawn by unspent balances lying with a 
number of Institutions.  As on 1
st December, 2009, maximum unspent balances 
amounting to Rs. 28.79 crore, Rs. 36.52 cror e and Rs. 80.43 crore were reported against 
the North-Eastern Indira Gandhi Regional Institute of Health and Medical Sciences, 
Shillong, LGB Regional Institute of Ment al Health, Tezpur, Assam and AIIMS New 
Delhi. Except AIIMS, the other two Institutes had unspent balances as on 1 st April, 
2009.  Reasons for availability of such a huge amount of unspent balances are not 
known to the Committee.  However, such a st ate of affairs in national level institutes 
is indeed a cause of concern for the Committ ee.  This clearly shows that some major 
activities both ongoing/proposed might be  adversely affected.  The Committee would 
appreciate if the matter is taken up with the concerned authorities without further 
delay.  
2.11 The Committee has been keeping track of the pendency level of utilization 
certificates (UCs) for the past several years. The Committee takes note of the fact that as 
per latest figures, as on 1.04.2009, provided by the Department, as many as 2091 UCs 
were pending which amounted to Rs. 2003.23 crore. The major defaulter States were – 

 10
Delhi (273 UCs amounting to Rs. 214.88 crore), Madhya Pradesh (66 UCs amounting to 
Rs. 191.73 crore), Tamilnadu (137 UCs amounting to Rs.  147.5 crore),  Andhra Pradesh 
(114 UCs amounting to Rs. 141.45 crore), Maharashtra (147 UCs amounting to Rs. 139.83 
crore), UP (109 UCs amounting to Rs. 135.54 crore) and Meghalaya (20 UCs amounting to 
Rs. 109.08 crore). In addition, there were 11  unreconciled UCs amounting to Rs. 148.77 
crore. 
2.12 The Committee takes note of the fact that the Department has taken remedial 
measures like for non-recurring grants, no fresh releases are being authorized unless 
audited Utilization Certificate for grant released in the year prior to the last year (i.e. 
with a ‘grace period’ of one year) has been received, subject to the condition that the 
sanction for the earlier grant does not permit a longer period of utilization. Further, the 
provisions of the General Financial Rules, which require that in case of recurring 
grants, funds for the same purpose beyond 75% of the next year’s provision will not be 
released unless audited Utilization Certificate for the grants released in the previous 
year is received, are being strictly implemented. Impact of such measures does not 
seem to be very effective as number of pending UCs continues to remain very high.  
Not only this, such a trend also indicates that schemes are not being implemented as 
envisaged.  
2.13 The Committee notes that as a further effort to maximize liquidation of UCs, a 
communication has been sent by the Secr etary (Health) to the Principal Health 
Secretaries of all the States. If need be, th is exercise needs to be pursued further with 
higher authorities.  
 
2.14 In response to another specific query regarding audit of various 
Programmes/Bodies, the Committ ee has been informed that 24  Autonomous 
Bodies/Societies/Grantee Institutions/Programmes were covered during 2008-09  and 25 
during 2009-10.  
 
2.15 The Committee appreciates this forward movement w.r.t. audit of Autonomous 
Bodies/Societies/Grantee Institutions/Programmes receiving Grants-in-Aid from the 
Department. The Committee recommends to the Department to furnish the major 
audit findings of 2008-09 and 2009-10 and actio n taken thereon. A list of Autonomous 

 11
Bodies/Societies/Grantee Institutions/Programmes likely to be covered during 2010-11 
may also be furnished to the Committee.    
2.16 The Committee notes that the irregularities pointed out by the Internal Audit 
Wing in its audit paras concerning NVBDCP, Delhi, NIMHANS, Bangalore, ICMR, 
New Delhi and V.P. Chest Institute, Delhi University (for two paras 24 & 28) are yet to 
be settled.  The Committee would like to be apprised of the updated status in this 
regard.  
2.17 The Committee has been keeping a close watch on the level of pendency of 
Audit paras contained in CAG Report on matters relating to the Department. The 
Committee observes that around 60 paras are under various stages of pendency and 
some of them date back to the period 1998-2000. Committee’s attention has been 
especially drawn by the following pending Audit paras:

2.18 The Committee has for long been empha sizing the Department to make all 
conceivable efforts to strengthen its intern al audit wing as also take all corrective 
measures so that the laid down procedures are adhered to in letter and spirit at the 
outset. The Committee is, however, surprised to observe that pendency of internal 
Audit Paras continues to be on the high side.  Following details are self-revealing: 
 

 12

 The Committee fails to understand as to why more than half of the Audit Paras 
raised during 2007-08 and 2008-09 continue to remain outstanding. The Committee 
would like to have a Status Note on the details of major units showing pendency 
alongwith instances of serious deviations from the prescribed financial norms (unit 
wise). 
 
III CENTRAL GOVERNMENT HEALTH SCHEME (CGHS) 
3.1 The Central Government Health Scheme (CGHS) is a scheme for providing health 
care to serving Central Government employ ees/pensioners and their dependant family 
members and certain other categories like Members of Parliament, freedom fighters, 
employees of some select autonomous bodies as  also PIB accredited journalists.  Initially 
started in Delhi, it covers 25 cities at pres ent.  As on 31-3-2009, there were 9,34,825 card 
holders with 31,81,719 beneficiaries.   
3.2 Status of allocation of funds and utilizati on thereof during the Eleventh Plan is as 
follows: 
 
                           (Rs. in crores)  
2007-08 2008-09 2009-10 
BE RE Actuals BE RE Actuals BE RE Actuals 
(as on 
 28.2.2010) 
40.00 40.00 35.20 50.00 59.37 45.45 51.00 66.64 40.92 
 
3.3 The Committee is unhappy to note the continuous underutilization of Plan 
funds during the first three financial years of the Eleventh  Plan, more so when the 
Department had sought an increase of funds during the RE stage during 2008-09 and 
2009-10 and it could not even utilize the funds provided to it at the BE stage. The 
Committee, therefore, strongly feels that the Department has to justify the need for 
additional funds by ensuring optimum utilization thereof and be careful in 

 13
placing/proposing its revised estimate so that funds are not unnecessarily blocked and 
go unspent.  
 
3.4 The Committee notes that the earmarked fund for 2010-11 un der the scheme are 
not in tune with the projected demands of the Department.  From the figures made 
available to the Committee, it is evident that  the reduction in allocation has been mainly 
on account of Materials & Supplies in No n-Plan where Rs. 159.85 crore have been 
allocated as against the requirement of Rs. 250.75 crore and Medical Treatment for 
Pensioners Head for which only Rs. 377.87 crore have been provided as against the 
projected demand of Rs. 627.00 crore. Simi larly, a shortfall of Rs. 5.33 crore under 
Materials & Supplies and Rs. 2.86 crore under IT Head under Plan allocation has been 
reported.  
 
3.5 The Committee does not find any logic for under provisioning for CGHS under 
Materials & Supplies as also for IT activ ities Head which are bound to affect the 
quality of services available to the CGHS beneficiaries. The Committee also observes 
that total tentative pendency with regard to medical claims of pensioners as on 
January, 2010 is Rs. 200 crore, including bill s pertaining to 2008-09.  In such a scenario, 
allocation of Rs. 377.87 crore for pensioners  against the projected requirement of Rs. 
627.00 crore for 2010-11 is bound to increase th e pendency further.  The Committee is 
of the view that full allocation of required funds has to be there so that pensioners are 
not made to face uncalled for hardships.  
   
3.6 The Committee is happy to note that a number of initiatives under CGHS are 
proposed to be undertaken during 2010-11. On e such initiative is expansion of a pilot 
project for Preventive Health Check-up for pensioners and serving employees over the 
age of 40 years at CGHS dispensary, Sector -12 and Sector-4, R.K. Puram, New Delhi. The 
Committee would like the Department to share its experiences of the pilot project and 
recommends to the Department to expand the initiative to more dispensaries in a time-
bound manner.  
3.7 The Committee also welcomes the intr oduction of a proposal under which the 
empanelled hospitals will forward the bill for the treatment of pensioner CGHS 

 14
beneficiaries to UTI-TSL and payment of the same within 10 days.  Such an initiative 
is bound to help the pensioners in availing of healthcare without any hindrance.  
 
3.8 The Committee is given to understand that the Department is coming up with the 
Central Government Employees and Pensioners Health Insurance Scheme (CGEPHIS) for 
Government servants and pensioners. Details of the proposed scheme are as under: 
• The Scheme is proposed to be implem ented on voluntary basis for existing 
employees and pensioners and compulsorily for future employees and pensioners. 
•  It shall provide coverage for meeting expenses of hospitalization and surgical 
procedures for beneficiaries up to Rs. 5.00 lakh per family per year on floater basis in any 
of the empanelled hospitals.  
• The beneficiary will have to pay an annual  premium, according to the grade pay, 
which will be in the range of Rs. 8,000/- to Rs. 12,000/- p.a. which is proposed to be 
subsidized by the Government.  
• Family will include self, spouse, two depe ndent children and dependent parents. 
New born will be covered as a part of insure d family member during the currency of the 
policy. Additional dependent family member can be covered by paying the fixed 
percentage of premium per additional dependent family member.  
3.9 The Committee views the introduction of the current proposal of the Ministry 
with serious concern. The Committee is no t convinced by Department’s categorical 
statement that it does not intend to replace the CGHS with this new insurance scheme. 
It is amply clear that the scheme would be mandatory for future employees and 
pensioners.  The Committee would like to point out that CGHS is one of the very 
important social security measures which ha s been providing health care services at 
economical rates to its beneficiaries for so many decades. The Committee fails to 
comprehend the rationale for depriving all the beneficiaries of such an essential 
service in the long run.  The Committee would like to point out that there is a well-
structured set up of CGHS dispensaries, Govt. Hospitals providing allopathic and 
AYUSH healthcare and also a number of empanelled diagnostic centres and private 
hospitals.  In the recent years a number of initiatives have also been taken for 
strengthening/revamping different comp onents under the Scheme.  A number of 
additional dispensaries are also proposed to be set up in the near future.  The existing 

 15
CGHS scheme is part of the social responsibility of Govt. for its serving/retired 
employees. Full reimbursement as per th e prescribed norms is payable to the 
beneficiaries. There is no upper limit on annual expenditure on the treatment of a 
beneficiary.  The very idea of capping the amount of coverage to Rs. 5 lakh per family 
per year as well as the limit of coverage to only - Self, spouse, two dependent children 
and dependent parents and thereby forcing for payment of the fixed percentage of 
premium per additional dependent family member under the proposed scheme 
indicates that the Govt. intends to dilute its social obligation primarily to its 
employees. This is unacceptable to the Committee.  
 
3.10 The Committee has for quite some time now been emphasizing for the 
introduction of two shift system in CGHS dispensaries. It had recommended starting it at 
least on a test case basis. However, as per the latest feedback given to the Committee, the 
Department has stated that starting of the two shift system has not been found feasible 
with the existing staff. 
 
3.11 The Committee is dismayed at the Depart ment’s approach from the very outset.  
First, it had taken the plea of a large number of private hospitals and diagnostic 
centres empanelled under the CGHS and the opposition from the staff side to any 
such move. Then, subsequent to Committee’s recommendation that flexible timings 
are order of the day and  desirable in the interest of many of the CGHS beneficiaries 
who are unable to avail the facilities of dispensaries in the morning hours, the 
Department in its Action Taken Note on Committee’s 27
th Report had stated that the 
Ministry intends to take the matter before the Committee of Secretaries. The latest 
reply proves beyond doubt that the Department is not willing to move forward in the 
matter. Like in the previous instances, the new plea taken by the Department is its 
inability to go ahead on account of its existing staff strength.  The Committee is of the 
opinion that if there is shortage of manp ower, necessary action plan may be drawn up 
and approval sought for requisite manpower at appropriate level. The Committee 
would like to reiterate that the matter needs to be taken up seriously and in the right 
spirit.  The Committee would like to be informed of the progress made in this regard. 
 

 16
 
3.12 The process of computerization under the CGHS which was initiated during 
2005 is continuing. The Committee notes that finally the Department has been able to 
finish the work of computerization in six cities i.e. Delhi, Kolkata, Nagpur, Pune, 
Chennai and Ranchi and it is nearing comp letion in Hyderabad, Mumbai and Patna. 
However, the Committee is constrained to no te that the Department has not been able 
to add momentum to the computerization wo rk even after the expiry of about five 
years as 15 cities still remain to be covered.  The Committee therefore recommends 
that the computerization work in the remaining cities be completed in a mission mode 
by the end of the Eleventh Plan.   
3.13 The Committee has been informed th at for successful implementation of 
computerization in CGHS facilities, onlin e high capacity UPS systems have been 
provided as part of the computerization package.  Training in Delhi has been provided to 
the staff by the National Info rmatics Centre (NIC) and Staff from outstation cities are 
deployed to Delhi for ‘hands-on’ training apart from local training sessions. 
3.14 The Committee appreciates the steps taken by the Department to neutralize the 
teething problems afflicting the smooth implementation of the computerization. 
However, the ground realities are not much encouraging. The Committee is informed 
that there is shortage of computer operat ors and the doctors have to first see the 
patients and then feed the relevant data in the computer system leading to delays and 
the resultant long queues. This way the computerization has only been able to help 
keep a check on the inventory in the store room and save time in putting up request 
for local purchases wherever required. Howe ver, it has not been able to obviate the 
problems faced by the beneficiaries particularly the old age pensioners who have to 
stand for long hours in queue.   The Committ ee, therefore, recommends that adequate 
trained manpower may be made available at the earliest.  
3.15 The Committee has been informed that  one dispensary for CGHS beneficiaries 
residing in Dwarka has already commenced fu nctioning. It has also purchased three 
plots of land in Dwarka for construction of dispensaries. CGHS dispensary at NOIDA 
has already started functioning from the ne wly constructed dispensary building. CGHS 
has already taken possession of plot allotted fo r construction of dispensary in Sector 5, 
Gurgaon. Building plan prepared by Cent ral Design Bureau, Directorate General of 

 17
Health Services has been approved by HUDA. CPWD has been requested to prepare the 
estimate of the cost of construction of the dispensary.  
3.16 The Committee is happy to note that after much delay the Department has 
finally been able to open a dispensary each in NOIDA and Dwarka. However, as the 
number of CGHS beneficiaries is very la rge in that area, the Committee recommends 
to the Department to put all its acts togeth er to start the three dispensaries for which 
plots have been purchased in Dwarka and one dispensary at Gurgaon.  
3.17 The Committee is given to understand that the workload on the dispensaries in 
Delhi has increased manifold. The Committee therefore recommends to the Department 
to have a realistic assessment of workload in CGHS dispensaries in Delhi with a view 
to explore opening up of new dispensaries  to mitigate the problems faced by the 
beneficiaries. The Committee would recomm end to the Department to furnish a 
detailed status note on the no. of beneficiaries attached with each dispensary, zone-
wise in Delhi and the NCR and plans of the Department to open new dispensaries in 
underserved areas of Delhi and NCR. The Committee may also be briefed about the 
availability of unused land lying with CGHS in different cities covered by CGHS 
alongwith the purpose of their acquirement.  
3.18 The Committee has been continuously vo icing its concern over the scarcity of 
manpower which is obviously bound to affect the quality of services being offered by the 
CGHS. The Committee notes the following status of manpower under CGHS, Delhi:- 
Sl. No.    Name of post        No. of Sanctioned Posts In Position     Vacant  
 
1.  Specialists   117         93     24 
2.  SAG      34         26     08 
3.  GDMO   667         612     55 
4.  Dental Surgeon    10         10     00 
5.  AYUSH     83         63     20 
 
3.19 The Committee also takes note of the large number of vacancies in different 
dispensaries across cities served by CGHS as indicated below: 
Group A :- 25 out of 94 in Chennai, 35 out of 116 in Mumbai, 18 out of 51 in Kanpur, 18 
out of 73 in Bangaluru and 32 out of 110 in Hyderabad 

 18
Group C :- 13 out of 66 in Jaipur, 26 out of 1 56 in Chennai, 26 out of 95 in Nagpur, 57 out 
of 280 in Mumbai, 22 out of 67 in Kanpur, 18 out of 57 in Allahabad 32 out of 116 in 
Bangaluru, and 120 out of 167 in Hyderabad 
  
3.20 The Committee has been given to understand that action has been initiated at 
various levels for filling up of vacancies in different categories of posts.  In the meantime, 
appointments on contract basis are being made. 
 
3.21 The Committee is not very happy with the element of adhocism in such a 
crucial area. The Committee would like to point out that the number of vacancies in a 
particular grade are bound to put pressure on the existing workforce thereby 
compromising with the quality of services provided to the beneficiaries. The 
Committee, therefore, recommends to the Department to urgently chalk out a strategy 
to fill up the vacant positions in all grades under CGHS in a time-bound manner.  
 
3.22 The Committee notes that  there is a laid down procedure followed for 
empanelment of private hospitals and diag nostic centers under CGHS. As per this 
procedure, CGHS notifies procedures / test s / treatment which can be availed of by 
CGHS beneficiaries in institutions and th e conditions applicable to selection of 
Hospitals/Centers for provision of selected  services. Tenders are thereafter invited 
seeking applications/bids from eligible parties.  Short-listed institut ions are inspected by 
the Quality Council of India which recommen ds institutions fulfilling the eligibility 
criteria.  Lowest rates for each procedure / test / treatment are determined. Eligible 
institutions accepting the terms and conditio ns (including the rates) are thereafter 
empanelled on submission of required performance bank guarantee. 
 
3.23 The Committee has noticed substantial variation in approved rates of 
investigations / treatment procedures in various metro cities. The Committee does not 
find much merit in the argument of the Department that rates offered by the private 
hospitals and diagnostic centres depend upon the prevailing market rates, number and 
business volume from CGHS beneficiarie s likely to be derived by eligible 
institutions/private institutions, which vary from city to city. The Committee is of the 

 19
opinion that substantial variation of 50 to 100 per cent in approved rates and that too in 
cities like Delhi, Mumbai, Chennai and Kolk ata cannot be considered commensurate 
with the variation in economic dynamics. The Committee accordingly, recommends to 
the Department to come up with a strategy to counter this variation of rates by having 
minimum disparities in the rates. 
  
3.24 The Committee has been informed that the CGHS has again in itiated the process 
of fresh empanelment of private hospitals and di agnostic centres.  It has been stated that 
institutions with accreditation with Nati onal Accreditation Board for Hospitals & 
Hospital care Providers (NABH) and Nati onal Accreditation Board for Testing & 
Calibration of Laboratories (N ABL) (respectively for hospitals and diagnostic centers) 
will be considered for empanelment. Institut ions already empanelled have been directed 
to obtain NABH / NABL accreditation within  a period of one year, failing which their 
names will be removed from the approved list. 
 
3.25 Though the Committee appreciates Department’s initiative for ensuring quality 
service to CGHS beneficiaries and has no doubts over the professionalism of 
NABH/NABL, yet it does not find much merit in making it compulsory as a 
precondition for empanelment under C GHS. The NABL/NABH lay down certain 
norms and standards to be observed and on inspection, they provide accreditation to 
those who fulfill the said standards. There is no guarantee that once accredited, the 
Hospital/diagnostic centre will maintain those standards till the next inspection by 
NABL/NABH. Further, it has been observed that many of the reputed hospitals don’t 
go for accreditation under them. In such a scenario it may be counterproductive as the 
beneficiaries may be deprived of services  under such reputed Hospitals/diagnostic 
centres. The Committee accordingly recommends to the Department to be cautious on 
this move.  
 
3.26 The Committee would also like to have  information regarding the number of 
NABH/NABL accredited Govt. hospitals/diagnostic centres as well as private 
hospitals/diagnostic centres empanelled under CGHS. 
 

 20
3.27 The Committee has been voicing its co ncern about the hardships being faced by 
cancer patients due to reputed specialized ho spitals in Delhi not opting for empanelment 
under CGHS.  Such patients have been permitted to take treatment in any multi-
speciality general purpose hospital subject to  the condition that reimbursement will be 
limited to actual expenditure or the rates noti fied in 2001, whichever is lower. CGHS has 
also now empanelled one hospital, viz. M/s SMH Curie Centre [Shanti Mukund 
Hospital], Vikas Marg, Karkardooma, Delhi fo r offering treatment to cancer patients. 
Further, the Department has stated that it is hopeful that the rates that would emerge 
from the fresh tender process will be realistic and acceptable to major hospitals. 
 
3.28 The Committee would like to point out that prior to 2001, all the premier 
specialized Hospitals had opted for CGHS empanelment.  The Committee wonders as 
to whether the recently empanelled Hospital of Delhi can provide the same services as 
being provided by Rajiv Gandhi Cancer Hospital or Dharamshila Cancer Hospital.  
The Committee is also not aware about the quality status of the only empanelled 
Hospital.  The Committee reiterates that the kind of specialized services being offered 
by a particular hospital, specially for terminal diseases like cancer and not the lowest 
rates should be the basic criteria for selection of a hospital.  One must not forget that 
the main objective of CGHS is to provide qu ality health care at economical rates. Not 
only this, Government is supposed to reimbu rse only the packages rates.  Payment of 
difference in treatment expenditure is the co ncern of the beneficiary.  In Committee’s 
view, package rates need to be governed by quality service.  Nobody stops the 
concerned authorities to negotiate with the applicant hospitals for arriving at mutually 
acceptable package rates. It cannot be that services offered by a small hospital can be 
considered on an equal footing with an advanced well established hospital.  The 
Committee, accordingly, recommends framing of a policy for revision of rates after 
every two years to realistically fix the ra tes of treatment. The Committee may be 
informed about the action taken by the Department in this regard. 
 
3.29 The Committee has been informed that Local Advisory Committees, consisting of 
representatives of resident welfare associat ions; pensioners’ associations; and Area 
Welfare Officer appointed by the Departme nt of Personnel & Training have been 

 21
constituted at each dispensary level, which me ets at least once in a month, preferably on 
the second Saturday of the month to attend to grievances of the beneficiaries. A 
complaint / grievance box and complaint / grieva nce register is also required to be kept 
at every dispensary. CGHS has also set up help line wherein any beneficiary can lodge 
his / her complaint, which is attended to by the concerned authorities and a reply sent in 
a time-bound manner. Claims adalats are being held annually in Delhi in each zone to 
settle any pending / outstanding bills of pensioner beneficiaries.  All Additional 
Directors / Joint Directors in CGHS cities out side Delhi have also been directed to hold 
Claims Adalat. 
 
3.30 The Committee appreciates the number of initiatives taken by the Department 
for effective functioning of CGHS. The Committee however, feels that wide publicity 
through local and print media needs to be given in this regard.  Only then CGHS 
beneficiaries will be in a position to avail of such facilities.   
 
IV SAFDARJUNG HOSPITAL AND VARDHMAN MAHAVEER MEDICAL 
COLLEGE, NEW DELHI 
 
4.1 Safdarjung Hospital, with a total bed st r e n g t h  o f  1 5 3 1 ,  p r o v i d e s  m e d i c a l  c a r e  t o  
millions of citizens from Delhi and the neig hboring States. Plan allocation of Rs. 69.00 
crore for 2009-10 for the Hospital was raised to  Rs. 129.92 crore at the RE stage, keeping 
in view funds required for payment of 6
th Pay Commission arrears; procurement of 
essential items and Equipment and Machinery etc. However, expenditure reported upto 
28
th February 2010 is Rs. 112.76 crore.  
 
4.2 The Committee notes that against projected Plan requirement of Rs. 180.60 crore 
for 2010-11 for the Hospital, allocation of Rs. 118.60 crore has been made. Given the 
fact that out of the RE plan allocation of Rs. 129.92 crore for 2009-10, the Hospital was 
able to spend Rs. 112.76 crore upto 28 th February, 2010, with every possibility of full 
utilization by the end of the year, the Committee is unable to comprehend the reasons 
behind a huge gap of Rs. 62.00 crore between the projected and budgeted funds for 
2010-11.  The Committee, therefore, recommends that additional funds be allocated for 

 22
the Hospital at RE stage so that lack of funds should not hamper the re-developmental 
activities undertaken by the Hospital.  
 
4.3 Committee has been constantly voicing its concern about continuance of large 
number of vacancies in different categories of posts in Safdarjung Hospital and VMCC.  
The Committee finds that positio n remains unchanged. As on 31 st January, 2010, out of 
total sanctioned strength of 5116 posts in di fferent categories, as many as 639 posts are 
lying vacant. What is more worrisome is that strength of Resident Doctors reflects the 
maximum shortfall.  As many as 245 Resident Doctors are required to be in place out of 
total strength of 1214.  Group ‘A’ Gazetted posts is the second category most affected 
with 62 vacant posts out of total 383 sanctioned  posts.  Percentage of vacancies in other 
categories is also quite alarming.  The Commi ttee is not aware about the status of filling 
up of Group ‘A’ posts to be initiated by the Department.  But from its post experience, the 
Committee can only conclude that it wo uld be a long drawn affair. During the 
presentation of the Secretary, Department  of Health & Family Welfare before the 
Committee, the Medical Superintendant of Safd arjung Hospital drew the attention of the 
Committee to acute shortage of manpower, sp ace and equipments in the Hospital owing 
to tremendous patient load.  Against an intake capacity of 1531 since 1960, present 
average bed occupancy was 2100. Thus actual  requirement of the hospital for both 
medical and para-medical personnel was su bstantially higher than the sanctioned 
strength.  
4.4 The Committee is very concerned with the vacancy position in different 
categories of posts at the Hospital. This coupled with the admission of the head of the 
Hospital itself that the sanctioned strength is far below the practical requirement, 
presents a sorry state of affairs. The Co mmittee is of the opinion that vacancies 
irrespective of any category are not only directly or indirectly bound to have an 
adverse impact on the quantity and quality of the services rendered by the Hospital 
but also result in overstressing of the existing staff. Further, the vacancies also have 
the potential to affect the quality of education imparted at the attached medical 
college. The Committee is given to understand that in case of Resident Doctors, 
vacancies occur frequently and are filled regularly depending on the requirement. The 
Committee is, however, not able to comprehend as to why there is about 25% of 

 23
vacancies at any given point of time. At least the Department can take pre-emptive 
action so that it has the next batch of Reside nt Doctors available to  step-in in place of 
the outgoing Resident Doctors. Similarly, vacancies in Group A and Group B Gazetted 
posts are far above the justifiable limits.  The Committee accordingly recommends to 
the Department to take up the issue of vacancies seriously and on priority basis. It also 
recommends a review of the requirement of manpower vis-à-vis the sanctioned 
strength of the Hospital and college. The Committee would like to be informed about 
the progress made in this regard.  
 
4.5 Committee’s attention was also drawn to wards acute shortage of nursing staff at 
the Hospital. Non-availability of good accommodation for nursing staff was cited as the 
main factor responsible for such  a situation.  Existing staff qu arters at Srinivaspuri were 
considered to be too inadequate.  The Comm ittee was given to understand that another 
project was coming up at Dwarka. The Committee is of the view that provision of 
accommodation nearest to the Hospital is the basic requirement for all hospital 
employees, especially for the nurses rendering 24 hour services.  The Committee, 
accordingly, recommends that provision of required accommodation facilities for 
nurses should be taken up on priority basis.  Viability of such facility at the Hospital 
premises itself under the ongoing Redevelopm ent Plan of the Hospital needs to be 
looked into.  
 
4.6 The Committee is aware that the Redevelo pment Plan of SJH with an outlay of Rs. 
2000 crore has already been included in the a pproved schemes for the Eleventh Plan. The 
plan involves systematic and phased demolition of existing structures in the Hospital 
and simultaneously rebuilding  m u l t i - s t o r e y  t o w e r s  w i t h  t i m e  b o u n d ,  s t a t e - o f - a r t  
construction over a period of six years. The Committee has been informed that the 
Expenditure Finance Committee (EFC) has recommended the Redevelopment proposal 
for consideration of the Cabinet Committee on  Economic Affairs (CCEA) with a total 
project cost of Rs.4821.17 crore. It has been stated that as the project outlay exceeds the 
amount earmarked for the purpose in the Eleven th Plan, it will have to necessarily be 
spread over the Twelfth Plan. Before submission  of the matter to the CCEA, a view is to 

 24
be taken about the resources which can be deployed for the purpose having regard to the 
overall availability of funds for the health sector.  
 
4.7 The Committee is unhappy with the pace with which things are moving in 
respect of the Re-development Plan of the Hospital. The Committee in its 27
th Report 
had urged the Department to make wholeh earted efforts to expedite the procedural 
formalities regarding this. The Committee notes that the Department itself has now 
stated a timeline of six years for the entire work to complete. Keeping in view the 
inordinate delay already made since its inclusion in the Eleventh Plan, the Committee 
recommends to the Department to make all conceivable efforts so that the whole 
redevelopment work is completed within this period of six years. For this to happen, 
targets need to be set, resources placed  at the disposal in time and implementing 
agencies need to be made accountable to  accomplish the set targets from the very 
outset.  
  
4.8 The Committee has for several years now been monitoring the progress made in 
the projects relating to establishment of Renal Transplantation Unit in the Department of 
Surgery/Urology and In Vitro Fertilization (IVF) clinic in Obs. and Gynae Department of 
the Hospital. As per the latest feedback given by the Department, preparatory work to 
start the Renal Transplantation Unit has been  completed and registration of unit under 
THO Act has already been obtained from Dte.  GHS. Infrastructure work was under final 
process. Staff has been identified and training carried out. Site (2
nd floor of Old 
Cardiology Wing) for setting up of IVF Unit  has been inspected in August, 2009. Orders 
were issued for carrying out necessary renovations. 
 
4.9 The Committee would like to draw the attention of the Department to the 
following status of the two projects furnished to it two years back:- 
“The space has been identified and funds allo cated. Rs. 98.48 lakhs for the purpose have 
been placed at the disposal of CPWD for the renovation work. Equipments are under process 
of procurement and likely to be completely pr ocured within next quarter of 2008-09. Two 
Surgeons, one Pathologist and 15 Nurses have already under-gone training in Renal 
Transplant at AIIMS. As regards IVF Unit, a proposal has been submitted by the HOD, Obs. 
& Gynae for approval in principle.”  
 

 25
4.10 The Committee is extremely disappoin ted with the persisting slow progress 
made by the Department w.r.t. setting up of the Renal Transplantation Unit and IVF 
Unit. The Committee recommends to the Department to remove all bottlenecks in 
setting up of the two units at the earliest. The Committee would like to have a status 
note on the same inter-alia containing details regarding the original timeline set, 
reasons for inordinate delay, resultant cost escalation and fresh timeline set with 
special efforts taken to meet the timeline this time.  
 
4.11 The Committee notes that Plan allocati on of Rs. 15 crore for the VMMC was 
reduced to Rs. 10 crore at the RE stage.  However, the expenditure reported till 28 th 
February, 2010 is only Rs. 7.44 crore. The College has been allocated Rs. 7 crore under 
Plan head for 2010-11. The Committee has been  informed that the reduction in 2009-10 
was made on a realistic assessment of the need s of the institution. Going by the current 
year’s progress of expenditure, the funds allocated for 2010-11 appear to be sufficient.  
 
4.12 The Committee is constrained to observe that the allocated funds for 2009-10 
could not be utilised even after steep reduction at the RE stage. To add to this the 
Department’s reply that the reduction was due  to realistic assessment of the needs of 
the Institute, proves that the earlier estimate, at the BE stage was not proper. Moreover, 
the fact that the College could not utilize this reduced amount also makes it clear that 
there is some element of improper planning at some stage. The Committee, 
accordingly, recommends to the Department to have a realistic assessment of the fund 
requirement for the College at the outset itself. 
 
 V DR. RAM MANOHAR LOHIA HOSPTIAL, NEW DELHI 
 
5.1 Dr. Ram Manohar Lohia Hospital has been a centre of excellence in the health care 
under the Government Sector Hospitals for ov er 75 years. The Hospital has round-the-
clock emergency services. All the services  are free of cost except Nursing Home 
treatment and some nominal charges for specia lized tests. It is a 1055 bedded hospital 
providing services in almost all major specialities and many super-specialties.  
 

 26
5.2 The Committee notes that the Plan and Non-Plan allocations in respect of Dr. RML 
Hospital in 2009-10 were Rs. 76.30 crore an d Rs. 117.00 crore, respectively, which were 
increased to Rs. 94.30 crore and Rs. 127.38 cror e at the RE stage. Additional funds were 
required for the payment of 6 th Pay Commission arrears to the staff, for Trauma Care 
Centre, expenses on medicines, consumables etc. As on 5 th March, 2010, 99% of the Non-
Plan funds and 85% of the Plan funds were utilized. For the year 2010-11, the Hospital 
had projected fund requirement of Rs. 227. 46 crore (Both Non-Plan and Plan) against 
which provision of Rs. 213.94 crore (Rs.107 crore in Non-Plan and Rs. 106.94 crore in 
Plan) has been made.  
 
5.3 The Committee was informed that most of the projects have been executed as 
scheduled and there was no delay in the ongoin g projects also. The Hospital proposes to 
start a general Maternity Ward (to start with 25 beds).  Moreover, the construction of a 
New Casualty Building, multi-storey super speciality block after likely possession of land 
in the G point near Trauma Care Centre was also proposed. Operationalisation of Renal 
Transplant Unit, setting up of a Nuclear Medi cine Department, setting up of Centre of 
Excellence for Mental Health and face lif ting of the hospital due to Commonwealth 
Games are the other projects being initiated/executed during the financial year 2010-11. 
 
5.4 The Committee takes note of the fact that inspite of a lot of developmental work 
being undertaken by the Hospital, several of the items of work like setting up of the 
Renal Transplantation Unit have gone beyond the targeted time-line.  Concerned that 
time-overruns are certain to result in cost overruns, the Committee recommends that 
the Hospital chalk out a time-bound Action Plan for such projects. The Committee 
would like to point out that the emphasis should not only be on the optimal 
utilization but also on the judicious utilizat ion of scarce funds. The Committee at the 
same time recommends to the Department to ensure that the developmental activities 
initiated by the Hospital do not get hampered due to scarcity of funds.  
 
5.5 The Committee welcomes the proposal to start a general maternity ward, 
construction of a New Casualty Building, multi-storey super speciality block and 

 27
setting up of Centre of Excellence for Mental Health. However, the Committee 
recommends to the Department to keep a close watch on the progress of new 
initiatives from the outset so that they are accomplished as targeted. The Committee 
also recommends to the Department to take  all necessary steps for early possession of 
land in the G point near Trauma Care Cent re. The Committee may be informed of the 
progress made w.r.t. the new initiatives.  
 
5.6 In response to another query regarding the sanctioned strength vis-à-vis the in- 
position status of posts in the Hospital an d the attached PGIMER, the Department has 
furnished the following position :-

5.7 At the outset the Committee places its displeasure over the mismatch in the 
figures provided to it with respect to vacant posts vis-à-vis ‘sanctioned and in-position’ 
posts.  For example, in respect of Group ‘A’ CHS posts, sanctioned and in-position 
posts are 219 and 194 (including 20 contractua l posts) respectively while vacancies 
shown are 27.  Such variations exist in respec t of some other categories of posts also.  
The Committee would like the Department to explain this mismatch. The Committee 
is constrained to find a lot of vacancies across the grades. The Hospital is already 
overburdened with patient load and in such a scenario it is really a matter of concern 
that such a large number of vacancies are allowed to continue. The Committee is of the 
opinion that in the smooth functioning of the hospital services, all the posts whether 
they are in technical side, nursing side or in the clerical side, have their part to play. 
Vacancies beyond permissible limits in an y grade affect the overall patient care 
offered by the Hospital as well as resu lt in overburdening the existing set of 

 28
employees. Taking into account the increased patient load, the Committee 
recommends to the Department to take immediate steps for reassessment of the 
manpower requirement in the Hospital and fill up the vacant posts in the various 
grades at the earliest. The Committee may be apprised of the steps initiated by the 
Department to streamline the existing procedure of recruitment so that vacancies in 
the different grades are filled up at the earliest. 
 
VI ALL INDIA INSTITUTE OF MEDICAL SCIENCES (AIIMS) 
  
6.1 AIIMS was established in 1956 by an act of Parliament as an institution of national 
importance. It continues to be a leader in the field of medical education, research and 
patient- care in keeping with its mandate. 
 
6.2 The Committee was informed that Plan allo cation of Rs. 177.00 crore for AIIMS in   
2009-10 was substantially increased to Rs. 320. 51 crore at the RE stage.  Owing to the 
stage of preparedness of projects, ‘Plan’ allo cation for 2009-10 for the Institute was fixed 
at Rs 177 crore against the projected demand of Rs. 900.00 crore. Later, at the RE stage, 
estimate of Rs.  320.51 crore was projected by  the Institute for the purchase of high end 
equipments, capital works and spill over proj ects like Jai Prakash Narain Apex Trauma 
Centre and Center for Dental Education & Research, which was acceded to. The 
Committee however notes that as on 28
th February, 2010, expenditure reported under 
Plan head was Rs. 270 crore. 
 
6.3 Allocation of Rs. 800 crore and Rs. 400 cr ore each under Plan and Non Plan heads 
has been earmarked for the Institute in 2010-11. 
  
6.4 Expansion of OPD block and New Emer gency Ward, Teaching Block, Research 
wing, OT/ICU and Private Ward Refurbishin g, Air-conditioning of Main Hospital, 
Multi-level parking and Multi-storey Animal House and Site development of Ansari 
Nagar campus & Community Centre at AV Nagar are the major projects proposed to be 
undertaken during 2010-11. 
 

 29
6.5 The Committee is anguished to note that out of a total allocation of Rs. 1461 
crore for the Eleventh Plan, the Institute do es not seem to be able to utilize the 
earmarked funds for the Plan period during the first three years of the Plan period. 
The Institute has been able to utilize on ly Rs. 648.00 crore (as on 28-2-2010).  The 
Committee sees little hope for optimum utiliz ation of remaining funds during the last 
two years of Eleventh Plan. As a result, the initiatives/ongoing projects are not likely 
to be executed as targeted originally. Th e Committee observes that as many as 21 
projects were envisaged to be completed during the Eleventh Plan for which an outlay 
of Rs. 623.63 crore was made. The Committee is, however, dismayed to note that by the 
end of the first three years of the Plan, utilization reported is only Rs. 70.18 crore. The 
situation is worsened by the fact that work in respect of as many as 14 projects is yet to 
start. These projects include expansio n of OPD Block and New Emergency Ward, 
Burns Unit at JPN Apex Trauma Centre, Multi-level parking, Pediatrics Surgery. Out 
of total expenditure of Rs. 70.18 crore, Rs. 55.00 crore has been utilized for minor works 
in Hospital/ hostels/ residential areas of the campus. Thus, progress made in the 
remaining six projects can also be considered at the initial stage only. The Committee 
cannot remain a silent spectator to present circumstances as AIIMS is India’s premier 
Institute providing specialized health services to patients from across the country. The 
Committee is of the firm view that it is  high time that an effective monitoring 
mechanism is set in motion. 
 
6.6 The Committee has been informed that 300 acres of land allotted by Haryana 
Government in District Jha jjar in February 2009 is proposed to be utilized for the 
development of the second campus for AIIMS in order to decongest the existing campus 
by shifting the non-critical areas. The Se cretary, Department of Health and Family 
Welfare and the Director, AIIMS during their deposition before the Committee on 23
rd 
March, 2010 informed that there was a limit to  expansion of the services in the present 
campus. Therefore AIIMS was planning to expa nd various capabilities in OPD Block in 
Masjid Moth where the Institute has got 35 acres of land.  
 
6.7 The Committee takes note of the fact that AIIMS has initiated the process of 
making a composite Redevelopment Plan of its Masjid Moth, Ansari Nagar and A.V. 

 30
Nagar campuses. The work of preparation of redevelopment plan of A.V. Nagar 
campus has been entrusted to CPWD. The work of Ansari Nagar and Masjid Moth 
campuses has been entrusted to HSCC by the Governing Body on 23 rd January, 2009. 
The concept Master Plan was finally a pproved by the Proj ect Implementation 
Committee on 12 th May, 2009. The revised Master Plan as presented before the 
Institute Body in December, 2009 has since been submitted to NDMC for approval. 
HSCC has also been entrusted with the co nsultancy and project management work of 
the redevelopment of old OPD Block. 
 
6.8 On being queried on the performance  level of the HSCC, a Public Sector 
Undertaking, the Secretary, Department of Health and Family Welfare expressed 
unhappiness with their approach and admitted to the laxity on the part of HSCC in 
carrying out the assigned developmental tasks at the Institute.  
 
6.9 While appreciating the candid statemen t of the Health Secretary, the Committee 
fails to comprehend the basis for such a Company being allowed to be associated with 
carrying out tasks of such magnitude and of public interest. The Committee 
understands that a number of projects in different areas are being handled by HSCC at 
present. The Committee recommends that a St atus Note giving all the details of the 
various projects awarded to HSCC may be furnished to it at the earliest. The 
Department should take the issue very seriously and if need be, some kind of penalty 
clause may be introduced in the agreement itself to fix liability for any delay. The 
Committee may be apprised of the development in this regard.  
 
6.10 Committee’s attention has been drawn by a very disturbing trend of utilization of 
allocated funds by AIIMS. Director, AIIMS du ring his deposition before the Committee, 
informed that during 2008-09 and 2009-10 some Plan Funds were diverted to Non-plan 
for paying the Sixth Pay commission’s arrear s and various other allowances to AIIMS 
employees. As a result, development projects could not be taken up. Frequent changes in 
the plan projects also had a negative impact. The Committee would like to point out 
that diversion of plan funds to non-plan side is in direct contravention of fiscal norms. 
The Committee deplores such a development at  the premier Institute of the country. It 

 31
seems that this trend has been continui ng since long. The Committee wonders about 
the role of Internal Audit Wing and Financial Adviser of the Department. The 
Committee is also very unhappy with the non-provision of required non-plan funds 
meant for payment of arrears and allowances to the employees which are anticipated 
beforehand. The Committee would appreciate  if fiscal management at AIIMS is 
brought to order. The Committee cannot accept the diversion of plan funds meant for 
development projects to non-plan side. Urgent action needs to be, taken in this crucial 
matter so that this trend is not allowed to continue any further. 
 
6.11 The issue of implementation of the Valiathan Committee Report has been 
engaging the attention of the Committee for quite some time now. The Committee set up 
on 11
th July, 2006 to study the functioning of AIIMS had submitted its Report on              
10th October, 2006.  It has taken more than  three years to complete the process of 
examination of recommendations by the Academic Committee, Governing Body and 
acceptance in principle of majority of th e recommendations by the Institute Body of 
AIIMS on 8th December, 2009.   
6.12 The Committee has been informed th at 31 recommendations not requiring 
structural changes and 7 recommendation s requiring amendments in the AIIMS 
Act/Rules/Regulations are at various stages of implementation.  Amendments requiring 
statutory amendments raise major policy issu es concerning the pattern of autonomy and 
the long term implications of major changes in governance structure of the Institute. 
These are being actively considered by a Hi gh Powered Committee and monitored at the 
highest level. Given the complexity of issues it is expected that the matter will require 
some more time. 
 
6.13 The Committee is happy to note that though inordinately delayed, finally there 
is some concrete action on the recommendations of the Valiathan Committee Report. 
The Committee would, however, recommend full implementation of the 
recommendations at an early date. The Co mmittee also hopes th at the process of 
examination of recommendations requiring  statutory amendments by the High 
Powered Committee will be completed at the earliest and implemented without 
unnecessary delay.   

 32
6.14 The Committee has been pursuing the issue of setting up of a Burns Unit at AIIMS 
at an early date. In response  t o  a  q u e r y  o n  t h e  p r o g r e s s  m a d e  i n  t h i s  r e g a r d ,  t h e  
Committee has been informed that the pr oposal, initially approved by SFC, was 
considered by the President of the Inst itute during a discussion on AIIMS re-
development master plan and it was noted that a Burns Ward already exists at the 
Safdarjung Hospital, located next to the JP N Apex Trauma Centre. In order to avoid 
duplication of facilities, it was felt that the proposal may not be pressed further. 
 
6.15 The Committee is unhappy with the dilly-dallying approach of the Department 
on the issue of setting up of the Burns Unit. The Committee notes with displeasure 
Department’s attitude from the outset whic h has been evident from the delay in the 
matter. ‘Avoiding duplication’ can never be  considered as a valid justification for 
dropping this project. The Committee notes th at funds to the tune of Rs. 48 crore have 
been earmarked for this purpose during the Eleventh Plan. The Committee is unaware 
whether the Department, before taking a view in the matter, has taken into account the 
patient load in the Burns Unit at the Safdarjung Hospital. Further, the Committee 
would also like to point out that availability of any service at Safdarjung Hospital 
cannot be made the criteria for not initiating the same at AIIMS. If that be so, the same 
argument can be put forth for not adding a particular service at Safdarjung Hospital 
since the same was available at the next door AIIMS.  The Committee would like to 
point out that with the ever increasing patient load, both the hospitals have been 
providing exemplary services. Any addition or expansion of service will be going to 
provide better healthcare to our people.  
 
6.16 The Committee notes that AIIMS is facing acute shortage of manpower at present. 
Out of total sanctioned strength of 9730 in different categories of posts, 1621 are lying 
vacant. The Committee notes that out of 6 29 Faculty posts, 190 posts are lying vacant. 
Similarly, Institute is facing shortage of 495 nurses. 
 
6.17 The Committee had in November 2008, re ceived a representation from Faculty 
Association, AIIMS, against the proposed mo ve of AIIMS authorities to earmark all the 

 33
existing 92 vacancies as reserved for SCs/STs/OBCs at all faculty levels - Assistant 
Professor, Associate Professor, Additional Pr ofessor & Professor, and in all specialties 
and super-specialties including Neurosurgery , Cardiac surgery etc. The Committee had 
subsequently taken up the issue with th e Department and has been constantly 
monitoring the progress thereof. The Comm ittee was informed that on this issue, a 
Committee was set up by the PMO under the chairmanship of Secretary, Coordination 
(Cabinet Secretariat) in February, 2009.  The Report of that Committee was duly 
forwarded to the Department on 22nd April, 2009. The Department thereafter approached 
the PMO on 30 th October 2009 to withdraw its earlie r direction of ‘kee ping recruitment 
process in abeyance’. 
6.18 Latest position in this regard is that an expert has been appointed to work out a 
comprehensive reservation roster for filling up  all the vacancies. The work of preparing 
such rosters keeping in view the Government  circular and orders as well as various 
judicial pronouncements was currently unde rway. During her deposition before the 
Committee on 23 rd March 2010, the Secretary, Departme nt of Health & Family Welfare 
informed that the report was expected to be submitted by 25 th March. The Department 
would be moving towards filling up the vacant Faculty posts, thereafter. 
6.19 The Committee is pained by the acute shortage of the manpower at the Institute 
and is of the opinion that the same is not justifiable in any manner. The Committee 
applauds the existing set of employees of th e Institute, who inspite of the tremendous 
work pressure, have been able to uphold the high standards and reputation of the 
Institute. With large number of vacancies at Faculty level posts, it is practically 
difficult to maintain the high standards of patient care and teaching in the Institute for 
which it has been known. The Committee accordingly recommends to the Department 
to make all out efforts to fill up the vacant  posts – both faculty and non-faculty and all 
other groups in a time-bound manner. 
 
6.20 In response to a query regarding Depa rtment’s efforts regarding checking the 
trend of doctors and nurses leaving the Government Sector /Country, the Committee is 
informed that only seven Faculty Members as against the total sanctioned strength of 629 
faculty posts (1.11 %,) have either resigne d or taken voluntary retirement from the 

 34
service of the AIIMS “on personal grounds” during the last one year. The number is, 
therefore, not abnormally high. Institute does  not have information about the number of 
personnel, if any, who left the country after quitting the service of the AIIMS.  
6.21 The Committee is not inclined to agree with the Department’s view. The 
Committee is aware that as per media repo rts during recent time some very prominent 
doctors of the Institute have left it citing pathetic working conditions. This coupled 
with the existing vacancies does not present a rosy picture of the Institute.  
 
6.22 The Committee would also like to invite the attention of the Department to its 
concerns raised in its 27th Report on Demands for Grants of the Department wherein it 
has cited a WHO report  according to which out of 428 students of AIIMS (studying 
between 1989 and 2000), 233 (54%) are residing abroad. This presents a very disturbing 
picture that on the one hand there is acute shortage of staff in the Institute and on the 
other hand pass outs from this one of the best training grounds in India are turning to 
greener pastures, that too after availing highly subsidized quality education. The 
Committee therefore reiterates it’s earlier stand that the Department should seriously 
come up with some kind of bond or legislation in respect of premier institutes like 
AIIMS so that the young graduates or post graduates are made to serve in AIIMS or 
other Government Hospitals/Institutes for a certain period. The Committee wishes to 
be informed of some concrete steps in this regard.
 
 
VII POST GRADUATE INSTITUTE OF MEDICAL EDUCATION & RESEARCH 
(PGIMER), CHANDIGARH 
 
7.1 The Postgraduate Institute of Me dical Education & Research (PGIMER), 
Chandigarh is a premier national centre of medical education, research, post graduate 
medical education and specialized hospital.  
 
7.2 The Committee notes that out of Plan  funds of Rs. 75.00 crore for PGIMER, 
Chandigarh for 2009-10, expenditure reported as on 28
th February, 2010 is Rs. 74 crore.  
As regards the Non Plan allocation, it was enhanced from Rs. 310 crore to Rs. 317 crore at 
the RE stage and the expenditure reported is Rs. 294.13 crore.    
 

 35
7.3 The Committee is happy to note that durin g the first three years  of the Eleventh 
Plan, there has not been a reduction in the Pl an funds at the RE stage. This is an 
indication that the allocated funds are being fully utilized by the Institute for its 
developmental activities.  
 
7.4 The Committee is given to understand that for the  11th Five Year Plan, an amount 
of Rs.625.00 crore has been sanct ioned for various projects of th e Institute. Out of this, in 
2007-08 and 2008-09, an amount of Rs. 70.00 crore and Rs. 60.00 crore, respectively were 
released and utilized by the Institute. In 2009-10, Rs. 75 crore have been released. The 
Committee notes that in BE 2010-11, Rs. 90 cror e have been provided as Plan grants for 
the Institute. In response to a query, th e Committee is informed that the present 
allocation of funds for 2010-11 is as per the projected requirements of the Institute. 
 
7.5 The Committee is constrained to note that the fund allocation during the first 
four years (including the current fiscal) amounts to less than half of the 11 th Plan 
allocation.  The funds allocated for the first 3 years of the Plan have been utilized 
optimally by the Institute.  The Committ ee is not aware about any new projects 
proposed to be taken up during the remaining period of the Plan. If that is not the 
case, the Committee can only conclude that th ere is some unrealistic assessment of the 
requirements of the Institute for the current Plan period leading to excess allocation of 
funds.  It is highly impractical to make out that the Institute can spend an enormous 
amount of Rs. 330 crore left for the final year of the Plan. The Committee would like to 
be updated in this regard.     
 
 7.6 The Committee notes that all the projects undertaken by the Institute during the 
Ninth and Tenth Plan are functional. The only exception  is the Advanced Trauma 
Centre (Phase-I) which is stated to be complete but not functional. The Committee 
would like to have the reasons for the abnormal delay in the operationalisation of the 
Centre. 
 
7.7 As regards the projects undertaken during the Eleventh Plan, the Committee 
finds that out of 14 projects, 6 are reported to be complete and functional.  The 

 36
Committee has been informed that the only major project with an estimated 
expenditure of Rs. 35.92 crore is the Advanc ed Trauma Centre (Phase II) which is 
likely to be completed by September, 2010. The Committee would, however, like to 
point out that the Centre needs to be made functional also at the earliest.  The 
Committee is not aware about the likely date  of Advanced Trauma Centre (Phase I) 
becoming functional. For a project completed in the previous Plan remaining non-
functional by the end of next Plan period  is indeed a cause of serious concern.  The 
Committee would appreciate if Advanced Trau ma Centre (Phase I) is made functional 
without any further delay. Simultaneously preparatory exercise for 
sanction/appointment of required staff and procurement of equipment for the 
Advanced Trauma Centre (Phase II) may also be initiated. 
 
7.8 Committee observes that out of the sanct ioned strength of 7013 posts in different 
categories, 906 posts are lying va cant. The Committee is given to  understand that bulk of 
the vacancies i.e. 152 vacancies out of 363 sanctioned posts in the teaching positions is at 
the level of Additional/Associate Professors which are to be filled on promotion basis. 
Positions at the level of Assistant Professors ar e stated to be largely on account of either 
recent creation of posts or non-availability  of suitable candidates from the reserved 
categories.  
 
7.9 The Committee is constrained to observe that nearly 50 percent of the vacancies 
are lying vacant at the level of Additional/Associate Professors. The Committee opines 
that the teaching standards at the Institute are being compromised with such a large 
number of vacancies. This situation can not be allowed to remain to persist any 
further. The Committee, therefore, recommends to the Department to go for special 
recruitment drives at the earliest to fill up the vacant positions at the Institute. The 
Committee may be apprised of the efforts put in by the Department in this regard.  
 
VIII NORTH EASTERN INDIRA GANDHI  REGIONAL INSTITUTE OF HEALTH 
& MEDICAL SCIENCES (NEIGRIHMS), SHILLONG 
 
8.1 The Institute has recently been established  in Shillong, on the lines of AIIMS, New 
Delhi and PGIMER, Chandigarh with the objective to provide specialised health care to 

 37
the people of North East region. An amount  of Rs. 1266.38 crore has been allocated for 
the Institute in the Eleventh Plan. 
 
8.2 The Committee notes that there has been  optimum utilization of funds allocated 
for the Institute during the first three years of  the Eleventh Plan.  A provision of Rs 67.85 
crore has been made for the Institute during 2010-11. In respect of physical achievements 
made by the Institute, it has been intimated that procurement of most of the ‘high end’ 
equipments, the process of which was pendin g for many years, has been completed. 
Further, second batch of MBBS students an d fourth batch (Degree Course) of Nursing 
Students have been admitted. Further develo pment plan of the Institute has also been 
finalized. 
  
8.3 The Committee would like to be briefe d about the components of ‘further 
development plan’ of the Institute. The Committee would also like to be given an idea 
whether the Institute has become fully operational and all the pending items of 
developmental work have been completed.  
 
8.4 The Committee has been keeping a clos e watch on the availability of the 
manpower for the Institute.  The Committee notes that as on 31
st December, 2009, out of 
the sanctioned strength of 1524 posts in diffe rent categories, as many as 684 are lying 
vacant. Position is very disappointing in all the categories as indicated below:

8.5    As regards the efforts made by the Department to fill up the vacancies at the 
earliest, it has been stated that despite addi tional financial benefits, there is an acute 
shortage of technically qualified personnel in  the North-East due to remote location and 
infrastructural deficiencies.  The Institute had launched a drive to fill the maximum 
number of teaching/faculty position and a recruitment process was set in motion. 
However, on a petition of some faculty me mbers, the Central Admi nistrative Tribunal 
(Guwahati Bench) stayed further process in October last year. The Institute Authorities 
are doing their best to get the case discharged or at least have the stay vacated.  
8.6 The Committee is very concerned at the continuance of a very large number of  
vacant positions  in the Institute that is supposed to be developed on the pattern of  
AIIMS, New Delhi and PGIMER, Chandigarh and provide  specialized0 health care to 
the deprived regions of North East India . The Committee notes that the efforts of the 
Institute to fill up Group “B” and “C” technical posts have yielded some fruits. But the 
same is not the case w.r.t. other categories of posts.  The Committee wonders as to how 
the Institute can be considered functional when more than one-third of sanctioned 
Faculty posts are lying vacant and out of total strength of 189 posts of SRD/JRD, only 
83 are in position. Both the teaching and healthcare facilities as envisaged are simply 
not there at present in the Institute.  The Committee is of the opinion that manpower 
alongwith the infrastructure is the foremost  important ingredient which is a must for 
smooth functioning of this Institute. The infrastructure having been put in place to a 
satisfactory level needs to be backed with requisite manpower. The Committee 
accordingly recommends to the Department to approach the CAT (Guwahati Bench) or 
an appropriate authority with a sense of urgency in public interest so that the case is 
settled or at least the stay is vacated to fa cilitate an early filling up of the vacancies. 
The Department needs to keep its relentless  efforts to attract manpower by special 
incentives and special recruitment drives. The Committee may be updated on the 
issue.  
IX CENTRAL INSTITUTE OF PSYCHIATRY, KANKE, RANCHI (CIP, RANCHI) 
 
9.1 The CIP, Ranchi is a pioneer Governme nt of India institution in the field of 
psychiatry and the only of its nature in eastern India. It was established in pre 

 39
independence era in 1918. The Institute has a be d capacity of 643. It provides diagnostic 
and treatment facilities in mental health and conducts PG courses in psychiatry. 
 
9.2 The Committee has been given to unders tand that the EFC for Rs.100.00 crore has 
been approved during the 11th Five Year Plan for the re-development of Institute and also 
to provide reservation for candidates of OB C category in the vari ous courses conducted 
by the Institute. The enhanced allocation of Rs. 21.64 crore and Rs. 27.25 crore at the R.E. 
2009-10 and B.E. 2010-11, respectively, has been  made to meet the above requirement of 
EFC. Plan funds utilized as on 28th February, 2010 were Rs. 19.41 core.  
 
9.3 The Committee has been voicing its co ncern over the stat us of the Premier 
Institute for the last few years. The Committee had noticed that re-development work of 
the Institute was being monitored by the Jhar khand High Court.  A writ petition (PIL) 
was filed by an NGO, namely, Jharkhand Hi ll Resource Development Society in 2003, 
against the Union of India and others regard ing inadequate facilities, vacant posts and 
m a n p o w e r  a t  C I P ,  R a n c h i .  I n  c o m p l i a n c e  w i t h  t h e  o r d e r s  p a s s e d  b y  t h e  H o n ’ b l e  
Jharkhand High Court, several schemes rega rding development of the Institute were 
submitted to Director General Health Services, New Delhi for their finalization and 
efforts were made to fill up the vacant post s of the Institute. The Hon’ble High Court has 
finally disposed off the PIL in its order dated 5
th May, 2008 with the direction to the 
respondents to fill up the vacant posts as expeditiously as possible with liberty to the 
petitioner to approach the Court again if there be lack of adequate response. 
  
9.4 As per the latest status note furnished by the Department in this regard, a 
proposal for creation of 140 additional post s (including 21 posts of Junior Residents & 6 
posts of Senior Residents) has been form ulated. The proposal, however, requires 
furnishing of some details for which the In stitute has been approached. The Secretary, 
Department of Health and Family Welfare during her deposition before the Committee 
on 23rd March, 2010 stated that the Department  would be able to fill up the posts very 
quickly.  As regards construction of 210 bedded hostel and 15 Married Residential flats, a 
rough cost Preliminary Estimate has been given by the CPWD which was under process 

 40
for according the administrative approval  and the financial sanction. Further, the 
Institute has been asked to submit the de tailed architectural designs for the OPD, 
Neurology Block, Casualty & Service Block, Residence Ward (Family Ward), Renovation 
and up-gradation of various wards, OT Bloc k, Diagnostic Centre, Pharmacy, CSSD Block 
and Corridors etc. 
 
9.5 The Committee notes with displeasure that the matter of creation of additional 
posts continues to be at a proposal stage ev en after about two years of directions given 
by the Jharkhand High Court. If this is the state of affairs inspite of a High Court 
intervention, the Committee is compelled to  conclude that things are not going to 
show any marked improvement in the near future. The Committee would like to point 
out that the Department has to play a more pro-active role in improving the services in 
a national level premier institute, especially because of High Court intervention. The 
Department must be aware that the area of services where CIP used to hold a 
commanding position in the earlier times is the most neglected area of our present-day 
healthcare. 
 
9.6 As regards the redevelopment plan, in spite of the proposal being approved way 
back in 2008-09, the progress w.r.t. the redevelopment of the Institute is at snail’s pace. 
The Committee would like to have a status note on the redevelopment plan of the 
Institute inter-alia covering the components of the plan, funds earmarked and spent, 
timeline set for completion alongwith the progress made so far. The Committee 
recommends to the Department to give all requisite support – in monetary terms as 
well as in terms of expeditious bureaucrati c clearances so that the redevelopment of 
the institute is achieved in a time bound manner.
