level and safai karamchari level. He also submitted before the Committee that the bed 
occupancy rate is more than one at any given point of time. The existing staff strength of 
the Hospital was overburdened due to the high bed occupancy rate at the Hospital.   
 
10.7 The Committee is very disturbed with the persisting shortage of staff in the 
college/hospital under the Department. The Committee recommends to the 
Department to make all conceivable efforts for an early filling up of the vacant posts 
so that the high standards of patient care are maintained. 
 
XI COMMON RECOMMENDATIONS REGARDING HOSPITALS 
 
11.1 The Committee is concerned with the procedural hassles coming in the way of 
the developmental work in various hospitals and institutions of repute under the 
Department. Though the Committee considers the laid down procedures as means of 
systemic checks and balances, yet it finds no merit in their coming in the way of 
urgent developmental work, and that too for a project targeted at taking care of the 
health care needs of the common man of the country. The Committee is of the 
considered opinion that the imperatives of development and environmental concerns 
need to be reconciled without further loss of time to avoid further time overruns in 
respect of this project.  The Committee th erefore recommends to the Department to 
have a centralized cell under a dedicated senior officer to coordinate all such 
procedural issues with the various agencies. The Department needs to play a proactive 
role here. If required, the matter may be taken up at the senior most level to facilitate 
early sanction of requisite statutory cl earances and the concerned approving 
authorities may also be apprised of the Co mmittee’s concern over approval delays in 
this regard.  The Committee may be informed about the progress made in this regard. 
 
11.2 The Committee is pained by the fact that the healthcare fraternity under the 
Department has to function under very stressful conditions.  Taking strong view of the 
enormous patient load in almost all the institutions/hospitals under the Department, 
which is in many cases manifold than envisaged, the Committee would like to make a 
general recommendation to the Departme nt.  It recommends strongly to the 
Department to have an independent reassessment of manpower done in all the 

 43
institutions/hospitals under it – sanctioned vis-à-vis the patient load of the hospital. 
The Committee also recommends a close mo nitoring of the vacancies in all the 
institutions/hospitals of the Department under a dedicated senior officer for 
coordination between the institutions/hospitals and the recruitment agencies so as to 
facilitate early filling up of vacant posts. 
XII OVERSIGHT COMMITTEE  
12.1 The Committee notes that the Department  is statutorily bound to implement the 
27 percent reservation for OBCs. Accordingly, a time-bound action plan was chalked out 
covering medical, non-medical and nursing courses. This is to be operationalized 
through the existing institutio ns directly under the contro l of Ministry of Health & 
Family Welfare. Allocation of seats amongst the institutions have  been identified and the 
expansion will be in disciplines where seats can be enhanced initially with the existing 
infrastructure by filling up vacancies agai nst sanctioned strength and creation of 
additional faculty and support staff.  
12.2 The Committee is aware that there is an approved outlay of Rs. 1827.00 crore for 
this scheme for the Eleventh Plan.  However, utilization status during the first three years 
of the Plan period has been negligible.  A pproved outlay of Rs. 600 crore was brought 
down to Rs. 100.00 crore in 2008-09.  However,  expenditure of only Rs. 79.66 crore could 
be made by the end of 2008-09.  However, in  response to a query regarding utilization 
status of Plan funds allocated in 2009-10, the Committee was informed that the final 
(revised) allocation of Rs.50 crore has been fully utilized. This statement of the 
Department is contrary to the figures of Rs . 16.90 crore furnished by the Department 
itself in response to another query. The Co mmittee has further been informed that the 
major activities under this head during the current year were of preparatory nature and, 
therefore, no physical targets were fixe d. However, two major milestones were - 
finalization of contract for the works exce eding Rs.300 crores for JIPMER, Puducherry 
and recommendation for clearance of project of Rs.454 crore by the EFC in respect of 
PGIMER, Chandigarh. Thus funds under this he ad were diverted for the time being to 
sectors needing supplementary funding.  

 44
12.3 On the justification regard ing increase in the Plan allocation to six times at Rs. 
300.00 crore during BE 2010-11 compared to the figure in RE 2009-10, the Committee has 
been informed that apart fr om major investments likely to be made by JIPMER and 
PGIMER under this head, expansion of infras tructure in the AIIMS to implement the 
recommendations of Oversight Committee will receive clearance of the competent 
authority. Accordingly it is expected that  the earmarked funds would be fully utilized 
and even more funds might be required later on.  
12.4 The Committee views with serious concern the non-implementation of the 
recommendations of the Oversight Committee.  The matter is more serious when the 
Department is apparently going for expansion only in those disciplines where seats 
can be enhanced initially with existing infrastructure with suitable augmentation of 
manpower. Funds do not seem to be a problem in the instant case as most of the 
approved outlay for the Eleventh Plan is still lying unutilized. Therefore, the 
Committee recommends to the Department to chart out a concrete strategy for 
implementing this constitutional obligation in a time bound manner and on priority 
basis. The Committee would also like the Department to explain as to how the 
expenditure figures of Rs. 16.90 crore (upto 28-2-2010) jumped to Rs. 50.00 crore in a 
matter of one month. 
 
XIII PRADHAN MANTRI SWASTHYA SURAKSHA YOJNA (PMSSY)  
  
13.1 Government of India had launched an ambitious scheme Pradhan Mantri 
Swasthya Suraksha Yojna (PMSSY), on 15th July, 2003, with the objective of reducing the 
gaps in the availability of tertiary health care across States by developing AIIMS like 
Institutions  in States  to cater to speciali zed  tertiary health ca re and also to augment 
facilities for quality medical education in the country. The scheme involves setting up of 
6 AIIMS like Institutions in Phase-I and 2 more in Phase-II. Another component of 
PMSSY is the upgradation of 13 Govt. Medical Colleges in Phase-I and 6 more in Phase-II 
by 2011-12. The AIIMS-like institutions ar e being set up in Patna, Raipur, Bhopal, 
Bhubaneshwar, Jodhpur and  Rishikesh. Each Institution will have a hospital of  850 beds 
providing medical treatment in 42  discip lines out of which 350 beds would  be 
exclusively for super  speciality and  ICU. These Institutes would also provide under-

 45
graduate and post graduate medical educat ion with admission capacity based on MCI 
norms.   
 
13.2 During the Tenth Plan, the outlay of  Rs. 999.00 crores for PMSSY was brought 
down to Rs. 385.00 crore and expenditure reported at the end of the Plan was only a 
meagre Rs. 20.00 crore.  The scheme has an approved outlay of Rs. 3955.00 crore for 
Eleventh Plan. The Committee however, notes th at trend of utilization of allocated funds 
has not picked up so far. 
 
13.3 The Committee notes that the Plan allo cation of Rs. 1447.92 crore was drastically 
reduced to Rs. 683.58 crore at the RE stage during 2009-10  and expenditure of only Rs . 
452.16 crore has been reported by the end of the year . Position was more or less same in 
the previous two years.  Non-awarding of ci vil works has been cited as the main factor 
responsible for the trend of utilization of allocated funds during 2009-10. 
 
13.4 Such a slow progress of implementation is bound to result in huge amount 
remaining unutilized at the end of the Plan period. The Committee had time and again 
been inviting the attention of the Department towards the urgent need to take some 
corrective measures, but it appears that the sa me has not yielded fruits atleast as of 
now.  
 
13.5 The Committee has been given to understand that against the projected demand 
of Rs. 1124 crore for 2010-11, allocation of Rs. 750 crore would not be sufficient to meet 
the requirements of activities under the PMSSY as the entire upgradation work and 
major portion of the civil work of the medical college and hospital complex would be 
completed in the financial year 2010-11.  It ha s also been pointed out that the estimated 
requirement of funds would be to the tune of Rs. 1700 crore. 
 
13.6 After an assessment of the progress of various components under the Scheme, 
the Committee in its 27
th Report had made its apprehensions very clear that with the 
way things were moving, it would take at least another Plan period before the six 

 46
AIIMS like Institutions would actually start functioning. After a gap of two years, the 
Committee finds that its apprehensions have proved to be correct. It is still a distant 
dream to see these Institutions functioning . The reason is that even after the 
completion of infrastructure related work , the Department has to concentrate on a 
whole lot of issues including manpower.  The Committee would like to point out that 
this ambitious project is of critical importance for creating much required tertiary 
health care facilities in the country. Under funding of the project should not be 
allowed to come in the way of access to quality and affordable health care for all.  The 
Committee, therefore, recommends that higher level of funds, as required, be allocated 
under this head at RE stage during 2010-11. 
 
13.7 In respect of a query regarding the ab normal delay in implementation of the 
project, the Committee is informed that process for selection of single Project Consultant 
and Architectural design concept for the 6 AIIM S- like institutions started in 2004 had to 
be abandoned. Whole process was initiated de novo in 2007 with each AIIMS site to be 
treated as a separate and independent project. It was also decided that the construction of 
housing complex be separated from that of the Hospital and Medical College so that it is 
constructed earlier. The Design DPR Consultants could be appointed only in October, 
2007 and Project Consultants in June, 2008.  
 
13.8 Status of six AIIMS like  Medical Colleges and Hospital Complex is as follows: 
 
 Scheduled Date Likely Date 
Selection of Contractors April, 2009 April, 2010 
Start of Civil Work September, 2009 June, 2010 
Date of Completion March, 2012 December, 2012 
 
During her deposition before the Committee on 23 rd March 2010, the Secretary, 
Department of Health & Family Welfare very candidly admitted to the procedural lapses 
on the part of the Department initially whil e implementing this scheme. She admitted to 
an error in decision making for selecting single Project Consultant and Architectural 
design concept for the 6 AIIMS- like institut ions which went horribly wrong. Secondly, 
she also admitted to lack of monitoring at an appropriately higher level. However, the 

 47
Committee was apprised that now the project is being strictly monitored dedicatedly at 
the level of a Joint Secretar y and the Secretary would be monitoring the progress every 
fortnight.  
13.9 As regards delay in construction of residential complexes, the Committee is given 
to understand that the reasons cited were ranged from removal of overhead electric lines, 
tree cutting to shifting of existing office and material by the State Government. The 
Committee takes note of the latest status of residential  complexes for the six Institutions:-

13.10 In the opinion of the Committee, the reasons cited for the delay are very flimsy 
in nature and indicative of ineffectiveness of monitoring and coordination 
mechanism.  The Committee is of the firm opinion that had  proper coordination been 
done with the concerned agencies of the St ates and if the Department had pursued the 
project vigorously towards achieving the set targets, it could have approached the 
State Governments at an appropriate high er level to thrash out State specific 
problems. The same is apparently lacking on the part of the Department.

13.12 The Committee has been monitoring the progress of implementation of PMSSY 
very closely.  The Committee observes th at scheduled dates of various stages of 
different components of both AIIMS like institutions alongwith their residential 
complexes and upgradation of 13 existing medical colleges have been revised time and 
again for varying reasons.  The Committee is perturbed to note that this ambitious 
programme was launched on 15 th July, 2003.  Even after a lapse of more than six years, 
the likely date for selection of contractors has been given as April, 2010, with civil 
work supposed to start in June, 2010 and date of completion projected as December, 
2012.  As per the latest information, bids  for the project are under finalization.  
Similarly, with regard to residential complexes, time-lines have been revised, 
indicating a delay of six months to one year.  The Committee apprehends that there are 
all chances of further revision of date of completion.  For example, whereas only 15 
percent and 20 percent of construction work in respect of Bhubaneswar and Bhopal 
residential complex, respectively, has been reported, their likely date of completion is 
December, 2010.   
  
13.13 Position with regard to upgradation of 13 medical colleges is also quite 
discouraging.  Here also with the exception of Trivandrum Medical College (reported 
to be complete and ready for inauguration), likely date of completion in respect of the 
remaining colleges indicates shifting 6 months to one year.  Here again in respect of 

 49
RIMS, Ranchi 15 percent progress, and fo r Srinagar Medical College, 39 percent 
progress has been made so for.  
 
13.14 The Committee appreciates the candid admission of the Secretary regarding 
errors of judgment of the Department and lack of adequate monitoring while 
implementing the Scheme. The Committee woul d, however, like to point out that this 
alone would not suffice. The new monitoring mechanism has to be in place 
immediately and every effort has to be made to adhere to the fresh timelines set under 
various components of the Scheme.  The Committee would also like to emphasize that 
the revised estimate of Rs. 820 crore for each AIIMS like Institution is more than two 
and half times when compared with original estimate of Rs. 332 crore.  Therefore, it is 
essential to adhere to the new time lines, otherwise there is every possibility of further 
escalation.  The Committee finds that PMSSY was conceptualized in the first year of 
the Tenth Plan.  As the things stand today, one can hope of its becoming operational 
only in the beginning of the Twelfth Plan.  
 
13.15 The Committee also recommends to the Department to start ground work in 
respect of the issues like requirement of manpower, machines and equipments etc. so 
that once the construction work is comple te, no further delay is caused in the 
operationalisation of the Institutes.  
 
13.16 The Committee takes a very serious view of the persistent non-compliance of an 
assurance given by the then Secretary during his presentation before the Committee 
during examination of Demand for Grants (2008- 09) of the Ministry to furnish a status 
report to the Committee regarding implemen tation of PMSSY project after every two 
months. The project is one of the landmark initiatives to enable the people of this 
country across states to have access to quality healthcare. Further, it involves enormous 
amount of precious tax payer’s money.  Therefore the Committee needs to keep a close 
watch on the progress of the Scheme. The Committee therefore reminds the 
Department to be careful and keep its assurance given before a Parliamentary 
Committee mandated to scrutinize its work.  
 

 50
13.17 The Committee also takes note of the fact  that the  Phase-II of PMSSY approved in 
March 2009 is yet to take off as the location  of the Institutes is under finalization in 
consultation with the State Governments of UP and West Bengal. Under Phase – II, it is 
proposed to set up two AIIMS-Like institutio ns in Uttar Pradesh and West Bengal. It is 
also proposed to upgrade 6 medical colleg e institutions - (i) Government Medical 
College, Amritsar, Punjab; (ii) Government Medical College, Tanda, Himachal Pradesh; 
(iii) Government Medical College, Madurai, Tamil Nadu; (iv) Government Medical 
College, Nagpur, Maharashtra and (v) Jawaharlal Nehru Medical College of Aligarh 
Muslim University, Aligarh (UP); and (vi) Pand it B.D. Sharma Postgraduate Institute of 
Medical Sciences, Rohtak. 
13.18 The estimated cost of each AIIMS-like institution is Rs.823 Crore. For upgradation 
of medical college institution, Central Govern ment will contribute Rs.125 Cr. each. The 
Expenditure Finance Committee approved the proposal on 2.12.2008.  Cabinet approved 
the Phase II of PMSSY on 5.2.2009.    
 
13.19 The Committee is happy to note that the second phase will extend the reach of 
AIIMS like facilities to more underserved areas of the country and will help in 
upgradation of other medical college institutions. However, the Committee cautions 
the Department to be careful from the outset  so that the problems that afflicted the 
first phase do not recur here. The Committee would like to be apprised of the progress 
made in implementing the second phase of the Scheme. 
 
XIV DEVELOPMENT OF NURSING SERVICES 
  
14.1 The objective of the development in nurs ing services is to maintain the pace of 
nursing education and nursing services with other developments in medical and 
technological services. Accordingly, the Co mmittee has been emphasizing the urgent 
need for expansion and strengthening of nursing services in the country.   
 
14.2 In respect of a utilization status of  Plan funds under various components, the 
Committee was inter-alia informed that allocation of Rs. 18 crore  provided in 2009-10  
was reduced to Rs. 2 crore at RE stage and the expenditure reported as on 28
th February, 

 51
2010 was only Rs. 0.50 crore. However, the Comm ittee is surprised to note that in respect 
of another pointed query regarding utilizat ion status of Plan funds during 2009-10 
earmarked for the scheme, the Committee has been furnished an altogether different 
picture. It was stated that at the time of finalization of RE, the modification/revision of 
the scheme was under process and therefore on  the basis of slow progress, funds were 
reduced drastically. However, subsequent to Government’s approval for implementation 
of the modified scheme and based on the pr oposals cleared by the Finance Division, the 
Department has enhanced the provision in th e final estimates to Rs. 15.00 crore by re-
appropriation. 
 
14.3 On a specific query regarding the Plan al location of Rs. 17 crore for the Scheme for 
2010-11, the Committee has been informed that the allocation of funds has been made on 
the basis of pre-revised scheme which will not be sufficient. As per the approved revised 
scheme the proposed detailed break up is as under: - 
(Rs. in crores) 
Training of Nurses 3.95 239 courses to be conducted 
Strengthening/ 
Upgradation  
83.50 94 institutions to be streng thened, 9 institutions to be  
upgraded into College of Nursing 
Nurses Award 0.80 Nurses Award Ce remony to be held on 12.05.2010 
Total 88.25  
 
14.4 When enquired about the poor progress w.r.t. to the implementation of the scheme 
during 2009-10 during Secretary’s deposition before the Committee, it was conveyed that 
there had been delay in getting the Cabinet approval. Now that the scheme has been 
approved the Department has released all the funds for various components earmarked 
for 2009-10. The Department also informed that more money would be required to 
implement the approved scheme. 
 
14.5 The Committee is not happy with th e abnormal delays in bureaucratic 
procedures of seeking approval. However, now that the new scheme has got the 
requisite approval of the Cabinet, the Committee would recommend to the 
Department to pursue the various components under the scheme vigorously. The 

 52
Committee also recommends to the Department to make a realistic assessment of 
funds requirement for various components and seek more funds at the RE stage.  
 
14.6 On an analysis of various physical targets set and their achievement level 
during the first three years of the Eleven th Plan, the Committee finds a discouraging 
scenario. Achievement in the most crucial component, i.e., training of nursing 
personnel has remained much below the the targeted coverage. Against a target of 
training of 8000, 9000 and 12000 nursing personnel during 2007-08, 2008-09 and 2009-10, 
funds could be released for training of only 2400, 2880 and 2800 nursing personnel 
during these three years. It is not known as to whether the reported number of nurses 
could be eventually trained. 
 
14.7 The Committee is not prepared to accept the stand of the Department that for 
both training and strengthening of institutions proposals have to come from the 
States. When the Department has started the scheme, and rightly so as per requirement 
of the profession, it has to take lead and responsibility to make it successful. It should 
motivate the nursing institutions to come up with proposals to avail the benefits under 
the scheme. Mere formal requests do not make any scheme successful. Concerted 
efforts need to be made by the Department in this regard.  
 
14.8 Another area of concern for the Committee has been strengthening of RAK 
College of Nursing as Centre of Excellence. Even before the beginning of the Eleventh 
Plan, this project is being shown as unde r progress. The Committee finds that the 
work for upgradation of RAK College of Nursing into Centre of Excellence had been 
entrusted to HSCC which has completed the work of renovation of existing building. 
However, the new construction has not ye t started due to pending clearances and 
approval of concept plan from various statut ory bodies. When asked on this specific 
issue during the meeting of the Committee on 23
rd March, 2010, the Committee was 
informed that there has been a problem due to the fact that the Department does not 
have the ownership of the land in favour of RAK College of Nursing. The matter has 
been taken up with the Department of Urban Development and the Land and 
Development Office and would be sorted out.   

 53
 
14.9 The Committee is not happy with the stat e of affairs of the work of upgradation 
of RAK College of Nursing into Centre of Excellence. This is the position when this 
old institution is located in Delhi itself.  The Committee wonders as to whether any 
attempt of monitoring has been made by the Department so far. The project has been 
in the pipeline for many years now. The Committee recommends to the Department to 
make sincere efforts to sort out all the outstanding issues and problem areas 
hampering in the upgradation of the College at the earliest. 
 
14.10 The Committee observes that acute shorta ge of nurses and high attrition rate of 
nurses are the twin problem areas coming in the way of availability of required nursing 
services in the country. In response to a qu ery regarding any specific initiatives taken by 
the Department so far, the Committee was in formed that the Indian Nursing Council has 
taken a number of measures to meet the shortages of nurses which include relaxation in 
various norms and conditions applicable in  nursing education both for students and 
teachers as well as for the institutions. 
 
14.11 Besides that the 6
th Pay Commission recommendations have led to improvement 
in the pay scales of nurses. 413 dwelling units at Sriniwaspuri have been built for nurses 
working in the Central Government Hospitals in Delhi viz. Safdarjung Hospital, Dr. RML 
Hospital, LHMC and Kalawati Saran Hospita ls. The Directorate of Estate has also 
allowed accommodation for Nurses in general pool. With all these measures the 
satisfaction level of nursing personal has been considerably increased. 
14.12     The Committee appreciates and we lcomes the aforesaid initiatives taken by 
the Department. The Committee infers that these measures will help in producing 
more number of nursing personnel. The Committee is happy to note that the 
Department has constructed 413 dwelling units for nurses and the Directorate of Estate 
has also allowed accommodation for Nurses in general pool. However, the Committee 
is still skeptical whether this would suff ice the requirements. The Committee would 
like to be enlightened about the availability of dwelling units vis-à-vis the demand.  

 54
14.13 The Committee has been very concerned about the need to keep a record of the 
number of nurses available in the country and accordingly it has been taking up the issue 
with the Department. As per the latest inform ation, the States of West Bengal, Gujarat 
and Maharashtra have already started upda ting Live Registers. They have been 
maintaining live register for nurses. The othe r State Councils have also been advised for 
maintaining up to date live register of nurses.  
 
14.14 The Committee is of the considered opinion that maintaining a Live Register of 
nurses would be of great help in formulating the various policies w.r.t. the nursing 
personnel. Accordingly, the Department needs to take up the issue with all the State 
Councils with some sense of urgency. The Department may also think of some kind of 
framework whereby the State Councils are persuaded to start maintaining a live 
register of nurses as also share the details with the Department to facilitate better 
policy formulation. 
 
14.15 Quality of training programmes (both pre and in service) for nurses and the 
functioning of Training Institutions for Nurs es has been another area of concern for the 
Committee. The Committee notes that the WHO,  in collaboration with Indian Nursing 
Council, has made rapid assessment of nurs ing training programmes in Bihar, Orissa, 
Rajasthan, Madhya Pradesh and Uttar Pradesh. According to its findings, the teacher-
student ratio, qualification and experience, physical facilities, adequacy of books in 
library are not as per INC norms. In addi tion, ‘Hands-on’ Training on conducting 
delivery were also found to be not satisfacto ry. It has further been stated that the same 
position prevails in the training programmes of other States as observed in the review 
taken by the Task Force consultative meeting on strategic frame work for XIth Plan on 
Nursing. The major reason for this drawback is lack of funding by the States. To address 
these issues, it has been decided to provide gr ant-in-aid of Rs.25.00 lakhs per institution 
instead of Rs.10.00 lakhs by the central go vernment for strengthening of training 
institutions. Similarly, the grant from centra l government has also been increased from 
Rs.75,000/- to Rs.1, 65,300/- per training cour se in training programmes during the XIth 
Plan period.   

 55
 
14.16 The Committee is very concerned with the shocking findings of the  nursing 
training programmes in Bihar, Orissa, Ra jasthan, Madhya Pradesh and Uttar Pradesh 
that came into light after the  rapid assessment made by WHO, in collaboration with 
Indian Nursing Council. The picture of other states is also stated to be no better. While 
appreciating the initiative taken by the Department, the Committee recommends that a 
strategy needs to be chalked out whereb y institutions getting grants from the 
Department are brought under some kind of regular training quality assessment 
schedule. This would not only help in plugging the loopholes but also help in 
devising better strategy for achieving desired training standards. 
 
XV NATIONAL MENTAL HEALTH PROGRAMME (NMHP) 
 
15.1 The National Mental Health Progra mme (NMHP) was started in 1982. Main 
objective of the Programme was to ensure the availability and accessibility of minimum 
mental healthcare for all in the foreseeable future, particularly to the most vulnerable and 
underprivileged sections of the population. 
 
15.2 The proposal for NMHP during the Eleven th Plan with an outlay of Rs.1000 crore 
was considered by the EFC in July, 2008.  Based on EFC recommendations, CCEA 
approval for three main co mponents of the Programme i.e., Manpower Development in 
Mental Health, Spill over ac tivities of the Tenth Plan and continuation of existing 
DMHPs  on existing norms with outlay of Rs. 473.445 crore was obtained and sanction 
for the same conveyed to the State Governments. 
 
15.3 The Committee observes that during the first two years of the Plan period, 
utilization of allocated funds was minimal. Against an allocation of Rs. 70.00 crore in 
both the years, expenditure reported at the end of year was only Rs. 14.57 crore and Rs. 
23.26 crore, respectively. Plan allocation of Rs. 70.00 crore in 2009-10 was reduced to Rs. 
55.00 crore at the RE stage. The expenditure reported as on 28
th February 2010 is Rs. 38 
crore only.  
 

 56

during 2007-08 and 2008-09 was also very discouraging as indicated below:

15.6 The Committee is aware that the slow pace of the implementation during the 
Tenth Plan has already resulted in the spillover of many components of the 
Programme to the Eleventh Plan. The Comm ittee is dismayed at the persistent 
discouraging level of achievem ents under the parameters, viz., the financial and 
physical, set under this ambitious Programm e. The Committee has been voicing its 
concern time and again in this regard and recommending to the Department to take 
immediate corrective measures to achieve th e targets set under the Programme. But no 
corrective measure seems to have been taken by the Department so far.  
 
15.7 In response to a pointed query in respect of the dismal performance under the 
various components of the Programme, the Secretary, Department of Health and Family 
Welfare during her presentation before the Committee attributed this to the lack of 
proposals and cooperation from the States. She also brought to the knowledge of the 
Committee the acute shortage of manpower especi ally of psychiatrists, stating that it has 
been a major hindrance in implementation of the Programme. Committee was given to 
understand that the situation was worsened by the availability of limited number of 
trained psychiatrists with a large number of them moving to private sector or abroad 
thereby resulting in human resource crunch under the Programme.  
 
15.8 The Committee is aware that active cooperation of the State Governments is key 
to the success of a Programme of vast magnitude like this. However, it cannot buy the 
argument that it is only the States who are responsible for not achieving the intended 
results under the Programme. The fact that EFC considered the proposal of NMHP 

 58
only in July, 2008 and only after that it was placed before the CCEA for approval is 
proof of the procedural ha ssles that are hampering the Programme. Further, the 
Department needs to take a pro-active approach and try to bring all the States on board 
for effective implementation of the Programme and have regular interactions with 
them.  It is high time that the problem areas hampering the implementation of the 
Programme are identified and suitable and timely action taken accordingly. 
 
15.9 As regards, the issue of shortage of manpower like trained Psychiatrists, 
Clinical Psychologists and Psychiatric social workers, the Committee shares the 
concerns of the Department. The Committee can only suggest that the requisite 
manpower needs to be attracted by offering a suitable compensation package and 
offering good working conditions. Another initiative which can be taken is to increase 
the number of seats in the field of Mental Health in the medical colleges across the 
country. The Committee would like to be briefed about the steps taken by the 
Department in this regard.  
 
15.10 The District Mental Health Programme (DMHP) is a vital component of the NMHP 
introduced in 1996.  The main objective of DMHP is to provide basic mental health services to the 
community and to integrate these with general health services. The Committee has been 
informed that as on date, 123 districts across the co untry have been covered under the 
District Mental Health Programme. Extens ion of DMHP to 200 new districts of the 
country with added components of Life Ski lls Training and Counseling in Schools, 
College Counseling Services, Work Place Stress Management and Suicide Prevention 
Services has been proposed in th e EFC proposal on NMHP for the 11
th Plan. The 
extension of DMHP is subject to the approv al of Expenditure Finance Committee and 
subsequent approval of Cabinet Committee on Economic Affairs. 
 
15.11 The Committee notes with displeasure that though the Department has 
proposed to include 200 more districts under this component during the Eleventh Plan, 
final approval is yet to be given by the competent authority. The Committee is 
informed that during 2010-11, the Department  proposes to implement DMHP on new 
pattern to only existing 123 districts.  

 59
 
15.12 Majority of districts in the country have not still been covered under District 
Mental Health Programme, which was initiated  way back in 1996.  It has taken almost 
15 years for extending the component to only 123 districts. With about 500 districts still 
remaining outside the reach of this vital aspect of the Programme, the Committee can 
only conclude that it has failed to take off so far. With the Secretary herself indicating 
a further delay, the Committee does not ha ve hope of more districts being covered 
under this component and of the Programm e in the near future. The Committee, 
therefore, recommends to the Department to seriously take all conceivable measures to 
cover the 200 Districts under DMHP, as envisaged in the Eleventh Plan period.  
 
XVI NATIONAL CANCER CONTROL PROGRAMME (NCCP) 
  
16.1 Cancer is stated to have become one of the ten leading causes of death in India. It 
is estimated that there are nearly 7 to 9 lakh cases occurring every year. Focus area for the 
Eleventh Plan period is early detection, diagnosis and treatment of common cancers 
especially among women that include cervical cancer, breast cancer and oral cancer.  
  
16.2 The Committee observes that, at present, NCCP is being implemented based on 
the norms of Tenth plan. The EFC of revised N C C P  f o r  E l e v e n t h  p l a n  i s  s t a t e d  t o  b e  
under preparation. Under the proposal, efforts are being made to provide basic cancer 
detection facilities in district hospitals, Community health centres and primary health 
centres. Also, it is intended to set up oncology wings in all medical colleges.  
 
16.3 The Committee has been witnessing a consistent unsatisfactory level of 
performance under the various components of  the Programme. Gap in physical targets 
and achievement level under different components of the Programme is evident in the 
Eleventh Plan period also. Neither the existing Regional Cancer Centres are being 
strengthened nor recognition being given to new Regional Centres. Shortfall is 
reported in the assistance being provide d to existing/new oncology wings of Govt. 
Medical Colleges. District Cancer Programme has also not been showing satisfactory 
progress.  
 

 60
16.4 Performance level of the Programme be comes all the more discouraging when it 
is noticed that there is no shortage of funds. Funds are being duly allocated every year, 
religiously reduced at the RE stage and the utilization status at the end of the year 
reported to be under-utilized. The Comm ittee notes the following figures of 
expenditure for 2007-08, 2008-09 and 2009-10 in respect of the National Cancer Control 
Programme (NCCP):- 
 
2007-08 2008-09 2009-10 (as on 28.2.10) 
BE RE Actuals BE RE Actuals BE RE Actuals 
140 70 46.32 120 120 33.60 95.00 35.00 14.24 
 
16.5 The Committee is dismayed at the fact that the Department does not appear to 
be serious w.r.t. the implementation of this programme and the same is evident from 
the fact that the EFC for 11 th Plan is being prepared by the Department and there is no 
approved scheme for the current Plan.   
 
16.6 The Department itself has been admitting cancer to be one of the ten leading 
causes of death in India with nearly 7 to 9 lakh cases occurring every year. The 
Committee opines that this Programme can be singled out as one of the classic 
examples where inspite of ample fund alloca tion and good objectives, the Programme 
has not been able to take off in the desired manner due to apparent apathy of the 
Department. 
 
16.7 The Committee is aware that the Depa rtment could utilize only Rs. 252.95 crore 
during the Tenth Plan out of an approved outlay of Rs. 333 crore. The Committee notes 
that the approved outlay for the Eleventh Pl an has been increased about eight times to  
Rs. 2400  crore out of which only Rs. 94.16 crore have been utilized upto 28
th February, 
2010. This leaves Rs. 2300 crore (approx.)  remaining to be utilized during the 
remaining two financial years of the Plan. Taking into account the provision of Rs. 155 
crore during 2010-11, it still leaves behind a huge amount of funds to the tune of Rs. 
2150 crore (approx.) left for the last financial year. With the current pace of utilization, 
the Committee apprehends that it is going to show a worse performance when 
compared with the Tenth Plan track record. 
 

 61
16.8 The Committee is very concerned with the persisting underutilization of funds 
earmarked under the Programme. The Comm ittee in its earlier reports has been 
commenting on this issue and has been recommending to it to take some corrective 
steps in this regard. The Committee cannot accept any justification in this regard as it 
is a clear indication of poor planning and execution as well as monitoring mechanism. 
The Committee hopes that at least now, the Department would take a more pro-active 
approach in getting all the requisite approvals without further delay and try to 
implement the revised Programme vigorously in the last two financial years with a 
focused strategy.  
 
16.9 In order to identify weak links and en sure effective implementation of various 
components of the programme, the Committee had in its 27
th Report recommended to 
the Department to give utmost priority to evaluation of the National Cancer Control 
Programme by a premier agency having experience of work in such Disease Control 
Programmes. However, in respect of a query in this regard, the Committee is informed 
that the programme was last evaluated in 2004 by the National Institute of Health and 
Family Welfare (NIHFW). Certain amendmen ts in the programme were incorporated 
in 2005, in the light of that assessment.  Another review has now become due. The 
process to select an independent outside agency has been initiated.  
 
16.10 The Committee is unhappy to note that the Department has actually not taken 
any step further in the last two years. The Committee would like to point out here that 
it was informed during examination of the Demands for Grants 2008-09 of the 
Department that the proposal submitted by the NIHFW for second evaluation of the 
NCCP was not up to the mark. In addition ICMR also submitted the proposal for 
evaluating the NCCP. It was stated that th e Department decided to invite proposals 
from other organizations including IIM. However, no response had been received in 
the matter and hence no final decision co uld be taken regarding selection of the 
agency for evaluating the NCCP. Th e Committee opines that the severe 
underutilization of funds as well as the ph ysical targets under the Programme warrant 
an immediate evaluation and necessary co rrective measures. The Committee therefore 
recommends to the Department to immediately evaluate the implementation of the 

 62
Programme so that all the loopholes and hurdles afflicting the smooth implementation 
of such an ambitious Programme are identified and corresponding corrective measures 
taken at the earliest.  
 
16.11 The Committee notes that a large number of Utilization Certificates are pending 
against various colleges spread across the country. Maximum amount of pending UCs 
is against the following colleges:-

 63
 
XVII NATIONAL TOBACCO CONTROL PROGRAMME (NTCP) 
 
17.1 The National Programme on Tobacco Cont rol (NTCP) aims to help implement the 
provisions of ‘Cigarettes and other Tobacco Products (Prohibition of Advertisement and 
Regulation of Trade and Commerce, Production, Supply and Distribution) Act, 2003’ and 
also to bring about greater awareness, sen sitize about the harmful effects of tobacco 
consumption and provide tobacco cessation facilities.   
  
17.2 The Committee is constrained to observe that in 2007-08 and 2009-10 BE funds 
had to be revised downwards substantially at the RE stage keeping in view the pace of 
expenditure under the Programme. Against an  allocation of Rs. 40.00 crore and 30.00 
crore in 2007-08 and 2009-10 respectively, an expenditure of only Rs. 13.98 crore and Rs. 
16.60 crore has been reported.The Committee opines that time and again the 
Department brings the rhetoric of scarcity of funds and here it is not utilizing the 
available resources persistently. The Commi ttee may be informed of the measures 
taken by the Department to optimally utilize the available resources under this 
Programme.  
 
17.3 The Committee observes that launched as  a pilot project in 12 districts of 6 
States in 2007-08, by the end of 2009-10, Programme has been extended to only 42 
districts of 21 States.  It would not be wrong to conclude that the Programme continues 
to be run on a pilot basis even after three year  of its launch.  This is the position when 
the target was to extend the coverage to 100 districts by the end of 2008-09. 
 
17.4 Committee’s attention has also been drawn by the progress made w.r.t. the setting 
up of the National Tobacco Regulatory Author ity. Proposed to be set up in 2008-09, the 
present position is that its setting up is unde r consideration of the Ministry. Further, as 
regards Referral Labs for testing of Tar and Nicotine contents in various tobacco 
products, the Committee has been informed that the Government has approved the 
setting up of tobacco testing labs under th e National Tobacco Control Programme. The 
Ministry will soon issue tenders for procur ement of equipment for the proposed tobacco 
testing labs. 

 64
 
17.5 The Committee would like to remind th e Department that subsequent to its 
examination of Demands for Grants of  the Ministry for 2008-09, in its 27 th Report, it 
had taken a strong view of the fact that despite the ‘in principle’ approval of the 
Planning Commission for setting up of the National Regulatory Authority and 
Referral Labs for testing Tar and Nicotine content and initiation of the process for 
seeking approval of the competent authority, no progress had been made for quite 
some time.  Accordingly, the Committee had called for concerted efforts of the 
Department to achieve the approval of th e competent authority for setting up of the 
National Regulatory Authority. However,  as per information available with the 
Committee now, there has been ‘no’ improvem ent on this front even after a period of 
two years. The Committee is dismayed at the Department’s attitude in this regard.  
National Tobacco regulatory Authority is supposed to be pivotal to the whole 
Programme to help in effective implementation of Tobacco free initiatives. Even the 
progress w.r.t. setting up of tobacco testing labs has not seen any improvement. To 
aggravate the matter further, the Depart ment has not been able to expand the 
Programme in more districts of the country as scheduled. Even the first Adult Tobacco 
Survey has been lingering on for quite some time. The Committee is upset with the 
way things are being managed under the Programme. Fixing realistic targets on paper 
is only the first step towards achieving a goal and the Committee does find the targets 
set to be realistic and achievable. Therefore,  it is not able to comprehend itself with 
the reasons for the delay in achieving the targets. The Committee would, therefore, 
recommend some urgent and concrete action plan in achieving the targets set under 
the Programme in a time bound manner.  
 
17.6 In response to a query in respect of the Department’s efforts to generate mass 
media/ IEC campaign regarding harmful effe cts of tobacco products, the Committee is 
given to understand that Rs 14.91 crore wa s released during 2009-10 for advertising and 
publicity activities.  The Committee also notes that an enhanced amount to the tune of Rs. 
23 crore has been earmarked for 2010-11 for this purpose.  
 

 65
17.7 The Committee is happy to note that the Department is focusing on Mass 
Media/ IEC campaign to generate awareness among the common people about the 
harmful effects of tobacco products. Awareness among the common masses, especially 
the younger generation, is the key to success of the whole Programme. The Committee 
would recommend that events like coming Commonwealth Games, venues of cricket 
and other matches being played in India, public gathering like the kumbh melas etc. 
may also be utilized to generate awareness among people with Mass Media/ IEC 
campaign at the sites. 
 
17.8 The Committee notes that the State Go vernment /UTs are the main enforcing 
agencies for implementing provisions of th e Cigarettes and Other Tobacco Products 
(Prohibition of Advertisement and Regula tion of Trade and Commerce, Production, 
Supply and Distribution) Act (COTPA), 2003. However, litigation in Supreme Court and 
High Courts against various provisions of COTPA and Rules made thereunder have been 
cited as a major barrier in effective implementa tion of the Act. To tackle such issues,  a 
Steering Committee has been constituted for taking suo-moto action with regard to 
violation of the Act. State Governments have also been advised to form State/Districts 
level Steering Committees. Inter-Ministerial ta sk force has been constituted to sensitize 
stakeholders on cross cutting issues of tobacco control and implementation of COTPA. 
Department has also stated that strengthen ing of legal support and establishment of a 
dedicated Tobacco Cell in the Ministry and co nstitution of National Tobacco Regulatory 
Authority will help in effective implementation of Tobacco free initiatives.  
 
17.9      The Committee notes that though  the Department has been able to come up 
with a law for restricting use and sale of the Tobacco products, the ground realities 
have not changed much. The various provisions of the Act are not being implemented 
in letter and spirit even in metro cities in India. Situation in respect of small towns 
and rural areas can well be imagined. The Department cannot remain a silent spectator 
on such vital issues. A proper pro-active st rategy should be developed whereby all the 
States are roped in to work sincerely in this regard.  
  

 66
XVIII            MEDICAL EDUCATION 
18.1 The Committee is given to understand that at present, there are 300 medical 
colleges in the country. Out of this, 229 ar e recognized medical colleges and 71 medical 
colleges which have been approved for runn ing MBBS courses will be considered for 
recognition as per the provisions of Medica l Council of India (MCI) regulations. The 
Committee is informed that while there is no provision for review of MBBS courses every 
five year, MCI carries out periodic inspection  of medical colleges as per the provision of 
IMC Act, 1956 and Regulations made there-un der to maintain the standards of medical 
education in the country. The medical co lleges which are found not meeting the 
requirement as per the regulations are given an  opportunity to rectify the deficiencies. In 
the recent past, MCI has recommended for with drawal of recognition in respect of 10 
medical colleges. Besides, MCI has also issued show cause notices to 15 medical colleges 
and sought compliance report. On receiv ing the compliance report from college 
authorities, MCI will verify the same  and, wherever necessary, submit its 
report/recommendations to the Central Go vernment for appropriate action. For the 
academic year 2010-11, recommendations from MCI are awaited in respect of 59 medical 
colleges for grant of annual renewal of perm ission for which the last date is 15.5.2010. It 
has also been informed that so far, 4 medica l colleges have filed court cases with regard 
to withdrawal of recognition.   
 
18.2 The Committee is concerned with the fact  that as many as 10 colleges are being 
considered for withdrawal of recognition and 15 more being issued show cause 
notices. From the large number of colleges caught compromising the standards while 
being scrutinized, it is clear that all is not well. This clubbed with the fact that these 
colleges are those which have come under scheduled scrutiny and there may be many 
which keep on functioning until they come under inspection scrutiny, makes the 
matter more serious.  The Committee would lik e to have a brief about the status of 
inspection of all the medical colleges inter-alia containing – the selection procedure 
vide which the colleges are selected for inspection, the date of establishment of 
college, date of inspection/s, shortcomings noticed and action taken during the last 5 
years. The Committee may also be informed of the details w.r.t. the progress made for 

 67
withdrawal of recognition of 10 colleges, show cause notice to 15 colleges and the court 
cases in respect of the 4 colleges.  
 
18.3 The Committee is given to understand th at as per Section 11(2) of the Indian 
Medical council Act, it is for the college authorities to apply for recognition to the Central 
Government through the relevant University  when the first batch of MBBS students 
appears for final year examination. Medical Co uncil of India carries out the inspection of 
the Applicant College and recommends to the Central Government to accord recognition 
to the medical degree if all facilities are av ailable at the medical college as per MCI 
norms. In respect of a pointed query regardin g the latest status with respect of medical 
colleges seeking recognition vis-à-vis their final batch of students appearing in the 
examination, the Committee has been informed that there are 15 medical colleges where 
the first batches of MBBS students have appe ared for final examination but are yet to be 
recognized in the academic year 2010. The recommendations in respect of these medical 
colleges are awaited from Medical Council of India.  
 
18.4 The Committee is not inclined to agree with the Department’s view that all is 
well with the existing procedure for reco gnition of MBBS degree colleges. The 
Department cannot escape from its responsibility by citing that it is the responsibility 
of the individual colleges to seek re cognition. The Committee opines that the 
Department needs to have into consideration the psychological impact of such a 
situation on the final year students who in its opinion would be more anxious to see 
their college getting recognit ion rather than taking their examinations seriously. The 
Committee is of the considered view that the whole procedure of recognition should 
be complete well before the final year  students take their examinations. The 
Committee therefore recommends to the Depa rtment to revisit its stand on the issue 
and come up with some concrete steps. A status note on the issue of recognition to 15 
medical colleges where the first batches of MBBS students have appeared for final 
examination in the academic year 2010 may be furnished.  
 
18.5 The Committee observes that out of 20,880 Bachelor of Dental Surgery seats, 1990 
remained vacant in 2007-08; 4347 out of 22,500 remained vacant in 2008-09 and 1651 out 

 68
of 23,570 remained vacant in 2009-10. The Committee also takes note of the fact that there 
are only 11,000 jobs created nationally in the Government Sector with  no projection of 
increase. However, the Dentists are eligible to practice privately and teach in private 
dental colleges. But the Committee is also aware of the limitations of the private sector to 
consume this supply.   
 
18.6 The Committee also notes that in 2004,  Dental Council of India proposed a 
moratorium on new colleges and addition of  new seats in existin g institutions for a 
minimum period of 5 years which was not ag reed to by the Government. The Committee 
is also informed that recently, the DCI has sent a proposal to the Government to make 
norms of opening new college more stringen t which includes replacing the 100 bedded 
hospitals in dental institution with an a ffiliation to an approved/recognised medical 
college in the interest of clinical traini ng and quality education, which is under 
consideration. Further, the Council has proposed to restrict the attachment of 
Government General Hospital with one dental college only irrespective of beds available 
there which is also under con sideration. Stringent criteria  for infrastructure and the 
requirements of publications for faculty have  been enforced. A central bio-metric system 
to monitor the presence of faculty on the day to day basis has also been introduced.  
 
18.7 The Committee is in full agreement with the initiatives proposed by the DCI 
especially in view of the fact that so many seats are lying vacant year after year. 
Further, the Committee is of the firm opinion that education and accordingly the 
number of seats should be need based an d keeping in view the demand and supply 
scenario. Here, by the figures available with the Committee, it is amply clear that there 
is an urgent need for intervention to bala nce the same. In view of the above, the 
Committee recommends to the Department to take some urgent steps in consultation 
with the DCI and the various State Governments, so that this unwanted trend is 
arrested at the earliest and precious resource s are utilized purposefully in other areas 
of medical education. The Committee also recommends to the Department to actively 
consider the proposals submitted to it by the DCI regarding making norms of opening 
new college more stringent.

XIX VACANCIES IN CHS CADRE 
 
19.1 The Central Health Service (CHS) cadr e comprises of four sub-cadres, viz., 
General Duty Medical Officers (GDMOs), Teac hing Specialists, Non-Teaching Specialists 
and Public Health Specialists. The Commit tee has been continuously keeping a close 
watch on the vacancies under the cadre. Following is the latest status of vacancies in CHS 
cadre:- 
 
                   
19.2 The Committee has been given to understand that constant efforts are being made 
to fill up the vacancies by regular mode of recruitment i.e. through UPSC by direct 
recruitment at entry level. DPC meetings are held regularly to fill up the vacancies by 
promotion method. Efforts are also being made to fill up the existing vacancies at entry 
level on contract basis temporarily.   As of now, a proposal for review of CHS Rules is 
under active consideration of this Ministry. As a result, requisitions for non-clinical 
specialties cannot be sent to UPSC till the amended CHS Rules are notified. In super-
specialties, in most of the sp ecialties, suitable officers are not found by the UPSC and 
thus the recruitment process proves to be  infructuous , which results in unfilled 
vacancies. It has also been observed that th e response of the candidates for vacancies at 
Andaman & Nicobar Islands as also at Lakshadweep Islands is not very encouraging, 
which leaves a gap for unfilled vacancies. However, this Ministry is making up the 
shortage by deputing Doctors from the mainland on rotation basis. 
 
19.3 The Committee would like to invite the attention of the Department to the 
following vacancy status in various cadres  in 2007 and 2008 furnished by it during the 
Committee’s examination of Demands for Grants of the Department 2008-09:-

19.4 The Committee notes with serious concern that apart from marginal 
improvement in GDMO sub-cadre, that too far too below any satisfactory level, there 
is seemingly no improvement in the filling up of the vacant positions across all the 
sub-cadres inspite of the sanctioned strength increasing in the three sub-cadres other 
than GDMO. The Committee is not convinced with the Department’s efforts because 
had the Department made sincere efforts there would have been some kind of forward 
movement in filling up the vacancies over the years. The Committee cannot make out 
the delay in making recruitments by UPSC. Promotion vacancies can be filled up by 
convening DPCs. Further, the Committee is unable to understand the delay in 
notifying the revised CHS rules especially when there is urgency involved in the 
matter. Temporary and contractual measures can never be a solution in the long run.

Committee would also like to be furnished a detailed note in respect of the posts 
under each sub-cadre, inter- alia covering issues like when it approached the UPSC for 
making direct recruitments, holding of DPCs, reasons for delay in notifying CHS rules 
and the efforts made by the Department for early filling up of the vacancies. 
 
19.6 In respect of a query regarding the st atus of proposals for –‘lateral entry of 
General Duty Medical Officers with PG qualification in the specialist sub-cadre’ and 
creating/upgrading 17% of total Senior Duty  posts in CHS for SAG level, the Committee 
is given to understand that the proposal for lateral entry of General Duty Medical 
Officers with PG qualification in Specialist sub-cadre has been included in the proposed 
amendment of CHS Rule-1996, and the same has been referred to UPSC for the approval. 

 71
There is no proposal for up-gradation of 17% of total senior posts in CHS for SAG level. 
However, the DACP Scheme has been extend ed up to SAG level post under which the 
promotion would be made to SAG post without linkage to vacancy. 
 
19.7 The Committee is happy to note that fina lly there is some respite in the offing 
to General Duty Medical Officers with PG qualification. The Committee recommends 
to the Department to pursue the matter sincerely so that the amendments are approved 
without further delay. The Committee would like to be apprised about the progress 
made in this regard. The Committee would also like to have the details of the DACP 
scheme.

XXI    MISCELLANEOUS 
 
21.1 National Essential Drug List of India (N EDL), notified by Drug Controller General 
of India (DCGI) in 2003 contai ning 354 drugs, is at present under further revision by the 
DCGI. 
21.2 The Committee opines that seven years is a long period and during this period 
several drugs would have become necessary fo r inclusion or deletion as per practical 
requirements. It is not happy with the delay in the revision of the List. It recommends 
an early updation of the List. The Committee may also be informed when did the 
updation process of NEDL started and whethe r there is a set time gap prescribed for 
initiating revision of the List. The Committee may also be informed of the progress 
made in this regard.   
21.3 The Committee notes that  “Whistle Blower Policy” has been announced with a 
provision of Rs. 3.50 crore by the Government  of India to encourage vigilant public 
participation in the detection of movement of spurious drugs in the country. Under this 
policy the informers would be suitably reward ed for providing concrete information in 
respect of movement of spurious drugs to the regulatory authorities. To give wide 
publicity to the initiative, the details of poli cy have been posted at the website of CDSCO 
(www.cdsco.nic.in
). 
21.4 The Committee is happy with the introduction of such a policy tendering 
monetary award to informers providing in formation about the spurious drugs. The 

 73
Committee would like to be apprised of the details of the policy along with any 
achievement made so far under the policy. 
21.5 The Committee takes note of a recent  Delhi High Court directive given on 23 rd 
March, 2007 whereby private hospitals whi ch were allotted cheap or free land by 
Government agencies are required to provide fr ee treatment to poor patients at the rate 
of 10% in the Indoor Patient Department  (IPD) and 25% in the Outdoor Patient 
Department (OPD) of their total respective treatment capacities. Hospitals to whom land 
was allotted on institutional/concessional rates have been directed to provide 10% of the 
total beds free for poor (whose family inco me is Rs.4, 000/- or less per month) and free 
treatment to 25% of total patients free in the OPD in pursuance of Hon’ble High Court 
orders dated 22.3.2007 and 17.7.2007. Three hospitals namely, Rajiv Gandhi Cancer 
Institute, St. Stephens Hospital and Mool Chand Khairati Ram Hospital are not providing 
free treatment stating that they are not covered under High Court orders. Ten other 
hospitals having 171 beds have approached Hon’ble Supreme Court against the orders of 
Hon’ble High Court and have got interim stay with regard to providing free X-ray, CT 
Scan, Diagnostics etc. However, they are prov iding free treatment as in accordance with 
orders of Hon’ble Supreme Court. Remaining 25 hospitals are providing free treatment 
as per direction of Hon’ble Supreme Court. The Committee may be informed about the 
progress w.r.t. the case filed by ten hospitals in Hon’ble Supreme Court.  
 
21.6 The Committee noticed that the bigger nations like USA are switching over to the 
generic medicines while India is still followin g branded medication pattern. India is the 
only country where generic branded and branded medicines are sold in the open markets.  
Either it should be generic or branded.  But in India, all big pharma companies are making 
generic branded medicines with manifold pric e tag in comparison to generic medicines.  
The Committee also notices that even if there is no difference of formulations, therapeutic 
use and the quality between a generic and branded machine, yet the branded and generic 
branded medicines are being sold at substantiall y higher prices in the open market.  Thus, 
the poor and the common men are forced to shell out extra money when they approach the 
chemist to buy such medicines which in the opinion of the Committee needs to be 
addressed with the sense of urgency.  The Co mmittee is further given to understand that 

 74
generic branded and branded medicines are be ing procured in all the Govt. hospitals, 
dispensaries and CGHS at substantially higher prices.  
21.7 The Committee is pained by the fact that  the poor patients have to pay for the 
branded name of the medicines.  This state of affairs is leading the poor patients to huge 
financial debts.  Besides, procurement of  the branded/generic branded medicines for 
Govt. hospitals, dispensaries and CGHS leads to heavy burden on the Exchequer 
comprising of precious taxp ayer’s money.  The Committee, therefore, recommends the 
Department to make it mandatory for the Go vt. hospitals and CGHS to procure only 
generic medicines so that their procuremen t cost is reduced substantially without 
compromising on the quality and efficacy of such medicines.  The Committee further 
recommends the Department to initiate some measures so that generic medicines are not 
marketed and sold as generic branded medicines leading to exorbitant prices by the 
profiteering companies so that such medici nes are available to the common man at 
reasonable prices.  The Committee also recommends that it should be made mandatory 
for the Government Doctors to prescribe on ly generic medicines. The Committee may 
be informed of the action taken by the Department in this regard.

DFG 2010-11   NRHM 
 
INTRODUCTION 
 
The National Rural Health Mission was launched by the Hon’ble Prime Minister 
o n        1 2
th April 2005, to provide accessible, a ffordable, accountable, effective and 
reliable primary healthcare services, especially to the poor and vulnerable sections of the 
rural population. The thrust of the Mission is on establishing a fully functional, 
community owned, decentralized health delivery system with inter sectoral convergence 
at all levels, to ensure simultaneous action on a wide range of determinants of health like 
water, sanitation, education, nutrition, social and gender equality.  
 
II. BUDGETARY PROVISIONS 
 
2.1 The allocation for Health Sector ha s been substantially enhanced from Rs. 
36378.00 crore in the Tenth Plan to Rs. 1,31,650.92 crore in the Eleventh Plan. Approved 
outlay for NRHM for the Eleventh plan is Rs. 90558.00 crore. The Committee observes 
that plan allocation during the first four years of the plan period shows a gradual increase. 
However, substantial plan allocation still remains to be released. Out of total approved 
outlay of Rs. 90558.00 crore for NRHM, total allocation for the first four years of the 
eleventh Plan amounts to Rs. 52,190.00 cr ore with a balance of Rs. 38368.00 crore 
remaining with only one year of the plan period left. The Committee is constrained to 
observe that shortfall has been reported every year in the utilization of the allocated 
funds. Utilization status has simply failed to keep pace with the upward trend in the 
allocation as indicated below:-

2.3 Not only this, with the exception of Mission and RCH Flexible Pool in other 
programmes, position has been more or  less the same during 2007-08 and 2008-09 
also. Committee’s attention has also been drawn by the fact that provisions for all 
these programmes have been made at an enhanced level in 2010-11 when compared 

 77
with 2009-10 allocations. The Committee wo uld, therefore, appreciate if concerted 
efforts are made by all concerns for opti mum and judicious utilization of allocated 
funds. If required, at the time of mid-ye ar assessment, the Department can pursue 
for additional funds with the Planning Commission and Ministry of Finance. 
 
2.4 The Committee views with serious conc ern the trend of allocation of funds 
and utilization thereof in some other crucial programmes such as National 
Programme for Control of Blindness, Io dine Deficiency Disorder Control 
Programme, Procurement of Drugs and Equipments under RCH, Information 
Education and Communication (Both RCH  & Non-RCH) and Area Projects are 
some of the other programmes under which performance level during the Eleventh 
Plan so far has also been far from satisf actory. The Committee has been repeatedly 
cautioning the Department to take remedial measures to ensure optimum utilization 
of funds year after year. However, no si gnificant improvement has been noticed so 
far. The case for Procurement of Drugs and Equipments under RCH remains a 
classic example, where, during 2008-09 Rs. 300 crore were allocated and no 
expenditure was made.  Again, in 2009-10 an initial outlay of Rs. 100 crore were 
made under the same head which was revised upwards to Rs. 150 crore and no 
expenditure is reported to be there up till January, 2010. Approved outlay of Rs. 
1500 crore for the Eleventh Plan has thus remained unspent in spite of three years 
of the Plan period being over. 
 
2.5 The Committee strongly feel that poor planning and execution on the part of 
the Department regarding the various on-going schemes have been responsible for 
such a discouraging scenario. The Committee is well aware of the fact that in a vast 
country like ours, with healthcare facilities specially in the rural areas showing a 
negligible presence, need for additional fu nd will also be there.  The Committee has 
also been strongly advocating the case fo r enhanced allocation of funds for NRHM 
but the trend of under-utilization/ unutilis ation weakens the justification for higher 
provision of funds. The Committee is also aware of the fact that Union Government 
alone cannot achieve the targeted goals. It has to carry out the implementation with 
the active support of State Governments. The Committee, therefore, once again 
recommends the Department to make its monitoring mechanism more effective so 
that such large amount of funds do not lapse/remain unutilized. The Committee also 

 78
suggests the Department to undertake a comprehensive self-assessment study on the 
means and methods used to improve financ ial accountability under the Mission so 
far, and based on the findings, to devise  a mechanism whereby funds utilization for 
the rest of the Mission period could be in a better state. Taking note of significant 
and persistent shortfall in utilization of the budgeted funds, the Committee would 
like the Department to exercise greater prudence in their budget formulation and 
make their estimates only after proper assessment and planning. 
 
 2.6 NRHM is targeted to provide accessible, affordable and equitable health care 
services for people living in rural areas of the country with focus on under-served 
population and marginalized groups. Therefore, it is the considered view of the 
Committee that              under-funding should not be allowed to come in the way of 
delivery of essential and quality health care  to the most vulnerable sections of our 
society. The Committee, therefore, recommen ds that additional funds be allocated 
under NRHM wherever required. 
 
2.7 The twin problems of pending Utilizati on Certificate (UCs) and Unspent Balances 
under various programmes/ components of NRHM have been engaging the attention of 
the Committee since long. The Committee finds that the year 2009-10 was also no 
different. The Committee was informed that as on 1
st April, 2009, there were 2083 UCs 
amounting to Rs. 7785.90 crore outstanding in respect of grants released upto March, 
2008. This pendency must have increased manifold by the end of March, 2010.  
 
2.8  On being asked specifically about the measures taken by the Department to 
liquidate pending Utilization Certificates, the Department has informed that as per the  
provision of the General Financial Rules it is necessary that in case of recurring grants, 
funds for the same purpose beyond 75% of the next year’s provision will not be released 
unless audited Utilisation Certificate for the grants released in the previous year is 
received. Further, for non-recurring grants, no fresh releases are being authorized unless 
audited Utilisation Certificate for grants released in the year prior to the last year (i.e., 
with a ‘grace period’ of one year) has been received.  
 
2.9 Besides that, this matter, apart from being regularly monitored in the Department, 
is also annually reviewed with the State Financial Advisers by Secretary (Expenditure) 
and the Union Finance Minister separately. And as a further effort to maximize 

 79
liquidation of UCs, a DO at the level of Secretary (Health) has been issued to all the 
Principal Health Secretaries of the States co ncerned. Lastly, with the intent to improve 
financial accountability, Financial Monito ring Groups comprising of MBAs, CAs, 
Accounts & Finance Managers are being deployed at the State-level under NRHM. 
 
2.10 The Committee observes that 2083 num ber of pending UCs amounting to Rs. 
7785.00 crore is too large to be comfortable with. The Committee notes that the 
biggest defaulters in terms of amount involved in pending UCs are Uttar Pradesh 
(Rs. 1097.39 crore), Karnataka  (Rs. 596.08 crore), Bihar (Rs. 537.74 crore) and 
Assam (437.11 crore) and unreconciled amount is to the tune of Rs. 707.40 crore 
involved in 31 UCs. The Committee is also astonished to find that the pendency of 
UCs has been continuing year after year, esp ecially in case of the High Focus States,  
EAG States and NE Region, although well ov er two-thirds of the time period of 
NRHM( 2005- 2010) is over. What is more astonishing is that  Union Territories like 
Delhi, Chandigarh, Puduchery and A&N Islands also figure in the list.  
 
2.11 The Committee would like to point out that mere advisories/ DOs to States 
for liquidation of pending UCs is not a tenable solution for expeditious liquidation 
of pending UCs. The Committee is of the view that monitoring of pending UCs 
needs to be further strengthened. The Committee also recommends that a senior 
officer be assigned the task of laising wi th the States and monitor the progress in 
this regard on a quarterly basis. The Co mmittee would like to be apprised of the 
action taken to revamp the monitoring of pending UCs. The Committee is also of the 
view that the Department take up the matter with the States showing maximum 
pendency of UCs at the highest level impressing upon them the imperative of 
furnishing the UCs timely and exercising fiscal discipline. 
 
 
2.12 The Committee observes that availability of unspent balances continues to remain 
on the high side, both under Mission Flexible Pool and RCH Flexible Pool, the two major 
components under NRHM. Opening balance as on 01.04.2008 with different State 
Governments/ UTs under Mission Flexible Pool was Rs. 4150.07 crore, while funds 
provided during the year 2008-09 were             Rs. 2597.44 crore. Against the total funds 
amounting to Rs. 6747.51 crore with the state,  utilization figures are only Rs. 3254.29 
crore, less than half of the total availabi lity of funds. The Committee finds that final 

 80

Rs. 3493.22 crore was again lying with the State Governments. Following are the States/

releases made during the year was very low:
